"""
tasks/tasks.py
───────────────
Celery task definitions for the lead processing pipeline. (HARDENED edition)

Two tasks:
  1. process_lead(lead_id, icp_prompt)
     — Full Stage 1 → 2 → AI Enrich → 3 → Score → DB write pipeline
     — One task instance per lead, dispatched in parallel
     — Each stage is individually guarded so a single stage failure degrades
       to a safe default rather than aborting the whole lead.
     — Per-company debug log (raw JSON of every stage) to Logs/.
     — After enrichment, persists the scrape + dispatches async embedding.

  2. finalise_job(job_id)
     — Chord callback after all leads processed.
     — Marks the job completed (or 'failed' if NOTHING was processed).

Dispatch (from FastAPI route, after leads are inserted):
    header   = [process_lead.s(lead.id, icp_prompt) for lead in leads]
    callback = finalise_job.s(job_id)
    chord(header)(callback)
"""

import json
import logging
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.celery_app import celery
from app.database import db_session
from app.models import Job, Lead, ValidationResult, FinalScore
from app.utils.logger import pipeline_logger
from app.utils.company_logger import CompanyLogger

from app.validators.icp_validator import _s, validate_icp
from app.validators.web_researcher import research_lead
from app.validators.contact_verifier import verify_contact
from app.scoring.scoring_engine import score_lead
from app.utils.monitor import bind_context
# RAG ingestion hook (persist scrape + fire async embedding into Qdrant)
from app.tasks.embed_task import persist_and_dispatch_embed
from app.config import settings
import threading
import hashlib
import redis
from app.validators.web_researcher import WebResearchResult
_web_cache_lock = threading.Lock()
_web_research_cache: dict[str, object] = {}
_redis_cache = redis.Redis.from_url(settings.REDIS_URL, decode_responses=True)
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ── Safe helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _num(value, default: float = 0.0) -> float:
    """Coerce to float, never raising (fixes round(None) crashes)."""
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return 1.0 if value else 0.0
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        try:
            return float(value)
        except Exception:
            return default
    if isinstance(value, str):
        import re
        m = re.search(r"-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?", value)
        if m:
            try:
                return float(m.group())
            except Exception:
                return default
    return default


class _NullLog:
    """No-op per-company logger used if CompanyLogger construction fails —
    guarantees the pipeline never crashes on a logging problem."""
    def header(self, *a, **k): pass
    def stage(self, *a, **k): pass
    def scrape(self, *a, **k): pass
    def note(self, *a, **k): pass
    def error(self, *a, **k): pass
    def close(self, *a, **k): pass


# ── Task 1: process_lead ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@celery.task(
    name="app.tasks.tasks.process_lead",
    bind=True,
    max_retries=3,
    default_retry_delay=30,
    acks_late=True,
)
def process_lead(self, lead_id: str, icp_prompt: str):
    """
    Full pipeline for a single lead:
    Stage 1 (ICP) → Stage 2 (Web) → AI Enrich → Stage 3 (Contact) → Score → DB
    """
    with db_session() as db:
        log = pipeline_logger(db, lead_id=lead_id)

        try:
            lead: Lead = db.query(Lead).filter(Lead.id == lead_id).first()
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"Failed to load lead {lead_id}: {e}")
            return
        if not lead:
            log.error(f"Lead {lead_id} not found — skipping")
            return

        log.set_job_id(lead.job_id)
        log.info(f"Processing lead: {lead.contact_name} @ {lead.company_name}")
        bind_context(job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id, company=lead.company_name)

        lead_data = _lead_to_dict(lead)

        try:
            clog = CompanyLogger(
                company_name=lead.company_name,
                contact_name=lead.contact_name,
                lead_id=lead_id,
                job_id=lead.job_id,
                icp_prompt=icp_prompt,
            )
            clog.header(lead_data)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"CompanyLogger init failed for {lead_id}: {e}")
            clog = _NullLog()

        try:
            # ── Stage 1: ICP Validation ───────────────────────────────────────
            log.info("Stage 1: ICP validation")
            t0 = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
            icp_result = validate_icp(lead_data, icp_prompt,
                           job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id)
            clog.stage("STAGE 1: ICP VALIDATION", icp_result,
                       source=validate_icp, inputs=lead_data, started=t0)
            log.info("  ↳ Stage 1 output: " + _summarise(
                icp_result, ["icp_match", "icp_score", "icp_reasoning",
                             "decision_maker_reasoning"]))
            _save_validation(db, lead_id, stage="icp", result=icp_result,
                             score=_num(getattr(icp_result, "icp_score", 0)),
                             passed=bool(getattr(icp_result, "icp_match", False)))
            db.commit()

            # ── Stage 2: Web Research (distributed identity cache) ───────────
            log.info("Stage 2: Web research")
            t0 = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
            # Contact identity is part of the key: reusing company-level data
            # across people would incorrectly reuse person_verified.
            identity = "|".join((_s(lead.company_name), _s(lead.company_website),
                                 _s(lead.contact_name), _s(lead.job_title))).lower()
            cache_key = "ql:web:" + hashlib.sha256(identity.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
            cached_web = _get_cached_web(cache_key)

            if cached_web:
                web_result = cached_web
                log.info(f"Web research cache hit for '{lead.company_name}' — skipping LLM call")
            else:
                web_result = research_lead(lead_data,
                                           job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id)
                _set_cached_web(cache_key, web_result)

            clog.stage("STAGE 2: WEB RESEARCH", web_result,
                       source=research_lead, inputs=lead_data, started=t0)
            log.info("  ↳ Stage 2 output: " + _summarise(
                web_result, ["company_verified", "person_verified",
                             "industry_confirmed", "employee_count_confirmed",
                             "technologies_found", "growth_signals", "sources"]))
            _save_validation(db, lead_id, stage="web", result=web_result,
                             score=_web_score(web_result),
                             passed=bool(getattr(web_result, "company_verified", False)),
                             sources=getattr(web_result, "sources", None))
            db.commit()

            # # ── AI Enrichment ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
            # log.info("AI enrichment: cols 19 & 20")
            # t0 = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
            # enriched = enrich_lead(lead_data, web_result,
            #                        job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id)
            # if not isinstance(enriched, dict):
            #     enriched = {}
            # lead.company_description = enriched.get("company_description", "") or ""
            # lead.technologies_used   = enriched.get("technologies_used", "") or ""
            # db.flush()
            # clog.stage("AI ENRICHMENT (cols 19 & 20)", enriched,
            #            source=enrich_lead, started=t0)
            # log.info("  ↳ Enrichment output: "
            #          f"description={_trunc(enriched.get('company_description'))} | "
            #          f"technologies={_trunc(enriched.get('technologies_used'))}")

            lead.company_description = getattr(web_result, "company_description", "") or ""
            lead.technologies_used   = getattr(web_result, "technologies_used", "") or ""
            db.commit()


            # ── RAG ingestion (best-effort) ───────────────────────────────────
            try:
                persist_and_dispatch_embed(db, lead, web_result, clog=clog)
            except Exception as e:
                logger.warning(f"RAG ingestion skipped for {lead_id}: {e}")

            # ── Stage 3: Contact Verification ─────────────────────────────────
            log.info("Stage 3: Contact verification")
            t0 = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
            contact_result = verify_contact(lead_data, web_result)
            clog.stage("STAGE 3: CONTACT VERIFICATION", contact_result,
                       source=verify_contact, inputs=lead_data, started=t0)
            log.info("  ↳ Stage 3 output: " + _summarise(
                contact_result, ["email_valid", "phone_valid",
                                 "contact_quality_score", "email_notes",
                                 "employment_confirmed"]))
            _save_validation(
                db, lead_id, stage="contact", result=contact_result,
                score=_num(getattr(contact_result, "contact_quality_score", 0)),
                passed=bool(getattr(contact_result, "email_valid", False)
                            or getattr(contact_result, "phone_valid", False)))
            db.commit()

            # ── Scoring ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
            log.info("Scoring")

            existing_fs = db.query(FinalScore).filter(
                FinalScore.lead_id == lead_id).first()
            if existing_fs:
                log.info(f"Score already exists for lead {lead_id} — skipping (retry guard)")
                _mark_lead_finished(db, lead, failed=False)
                clog.close(existing_fs)
                return

            lead_data["Company Description"] = lead.company_description or ""
            lead_data["Technologies Used"]   = lead.technologies_used or ""

            t0 = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
            score_result = score_lead(lead_data, icp_result, web_result,
                                      contact_result,
                                      job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id)
            clog.stage("SCORING", score_result, source=score_lead, started=t0)

            fs = FinalScore(
                lead_id=         lead_id,
                total_score=     _num(getattr(score_result, "total_score", 0)),
                icp_score=       _num(getattr(score_result, "icp_score", 0)),
                authority_score= _num(getattr(score_result, "authority_score", 0)),
                data_score=      _num(getattr(score_result, "data_score", 0)),
                growth_score=    _num(getattr(score_result, "growth_score", 0)),
                contact_score=   _num(getattr(score_result, "contact_score", 0)),
                priority=        getattr(score_result, "priority", "Low") or "Low",
                reasoning=       getattr(score_result, "reasoning", "") or "",
                growth_signals=  getattr(score_result, "growth_signals", "") or "",
                outreach_intelligence=getattr(score_result, "outreach_intelligence", "") or "",
            )
            db.add(fs)
            db.flush()

            log.info(
                "  ↳ Score breakdown: "
                f"icp={fs.icp_score} authority={fs.authority_score} "
                f"data={fs.data_score} growth={fs.growth_score} "
                f"contact={fs.contact_score}")
            log.info(
                f"Done: score={fs.total_score} priority={fs.priority} — "
                f"{_trunc(fs.reasoning, 140)}")

            clog.close(score_result)
            _mark_lead_finished(db, lead, failed=False)

        except Exception as exc:
            log.error(f"Pipeline error for {lead_id}: {exc}")
            try:
                clog.error("Pipeline error", exc=exc)
            except Exception:
                pass
            try:
                raise self.retry(exc=exc)
            except self.MaxRetriesExceededError:
                log.error("Max retries exceeded — marking lead as failed")
                try:
                    clog.note("Max retries exceeded — marking lead as failed")
                    clog.close()
                except Exception:
                    pass
                _mark_lead_finished(db, lead, failed=True)

# ── Task 2: finalise_job ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@celery.task(name="app.tasks.tasks.finalise_job", bind=True, max_retries=1)
def finalise_job(self, results: list, job_id: str):
    with db_session() as db:
        log = pipeline_logger(db, job_id=job_id)
        try:
            job: Job = db.query(Job).filter(Job.id == job_id).first()
        except Exception as e:
            log.error(f"finalise_job: failed to load job {job_id}: {e}")
            return
        if not job:
            log.error(f"Job {job_id} not found in finalise_job")
            return

        processed = job.processed_rows or 0
        total = job.total_rows or 0

        # Aggregate token totals from llm_call_logs for this job
        try:
            from sqlalchemy import func as sqlfunc
            from app.models import LLMCallLog
            row = (
                db.query(
                    sqlfunc.sum(LLMCallLog.prompt_tokens).label("p"),
                    sqlfunc.sum(LLMCallLog.completion_tokens).label("c"),
                    sqlfunc.sum(LLMCallLog.total_tokens).label("t"),
                )
                .filter(LLMCallLog.job_id == job_id)
                .first()
            )
            if row:
                job.prompt_tokens     = int(row.p or 0)
                job.completion_tokens = int(row.c or 0)
                job.total_tokens_used = int(row.t or 0)
                log.info(
                    f"Token totals — prompt={job.prompt_tokens} "
                    f"completion={job.completion_tokens} "
                    f"total={job.total_tokens_used}"
                )
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"Token rollup failed for job {job_id}: {e}")

        # Record which models were used
        try:
            job.llm_model   = settings.AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT
            job.embed_model = settings.EMBED_DEPLOYMENT
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"Model name capture failed for job {job_id}: {e}")

        # If literally nothing was processed, mark failed rather than pretend OK.
        job.status = "completed" if processed > 0 else "failed"
        job.completed_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        db.flush()

        log.info(f"Job finalised ({job.status}): {processed}/{total} leads processed")


# ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _trunc(value, limit: int = 100) -> str:
    if value is None:
        return "∅"
    text = str(value).replace("\n", " ").strip()
    return text if len(text) <= limit else text[:limit] + "…"


def _summarise(result, fields: list[str]) -> str:
    parts = []
    for field in fields:
        try:
            if not hasattr(result, field):
                continue
            val = getattr(result, field)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if val is None or val == "":
            continue
        if isinstance(val, str) and len(val) > 80:
            val = _trunc(val, 80)
        parts.append(f"{field}={val}")
    return " | ".join(parts) if parts else "(no fields available)"


def _lead_to_dict(lead: Lead) -> dict:
    """Convert a Lead ORM object to the canonical column dict (None-safe)."""
    field_map = {
        "Contact Name":        lead.contact_name,
        "Job Title":           lead.job_title,
        "Contact Department":  lead.contact_department,
        "Contact Seniority":   lead.contact_seniority,
        "LinkedIn Profile URL":lead.linkedin_profile_url,
        "Email Address":       lead.email_address,
        "Phone Number":        lead.phone_number,
        "Direct Phone Number": lead.direct_phone_number,
        "Contact Country":     lead.contact_country,
        "Contact State/Region":lead.contact_state_region,
        "Contact City":        lead.contact_city,
        "Company Name":        lead.company_name,
        "Company Website":     lead.company_website,
        "Company LinkedIn URL":lead.company_linkedin_url,
        "Industry":            lead.industry,
        "Sub Industry":        lead.sub_industry,
        "Employee Count":      lead.employee_count,
        "Annual Revenue":      lead.annual_revenue,
        "Company Description": lead.company_description,
        "Technologies Used":   lead.technologies_used,
        "Company Country":     lead.company_country,
        "Company State/Region":lead.company_state_region,
        "Company City":        lead.company_city,
        "Company HQ Location": lead.company_hq_location,
        "HQ Phone Number":     lead.hq_phone_number,
        "CRM Account ID":      lead.crm_account_id,
        "CRM Contact ID":      lead.crm_contact_id,
        "Lead Source":         lead.lead_source,
        "Notes":               lead.notes,
    }
    return {k: (v or "") for k, v in field_map.items()}


def _save_validation(
    db: Session, lead_id: str, stage: str, result,
    score: float, passed: bool, sources=None,
) -> None:
    """Persist a stage result. None-safe on score + sources. Never raises hard."""
    try:
        vr = db.query(ValidationResult).filter(
            ValidationResult.lead_id == lead_id,
            ValidationResult.stage == stage,
        ).first() or ValidationResult(lead_id=lead_id, stage=stage)
        vr.passed = bool(passed)
        vr.score = round(_num(score), 2)
        vr.reasoning = (getattr(result, "decision_maker_reasoning", None)
                        or getattr(result, "growth_signals", None)
                        or getattr(result, "email_notes", None)
                        or "")
        vr.raw_output = getattr(result, "raw_output", "") or ""
        vr.sources_json = json.dumps(sources) if sources else None
        if vr.id is None:
            db.add(vr)
        db.flush()
    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning(f"_save_validation({stage}) failed for {lead_id}: {e}")


def _web_score(web_result) -> float:
    """Convert Stage 2 boolean fields to a 0–10 score (attribute-safe)."""
    try:
        growth = getattr(web_result, "growth_signals", "") or ""
        checks = [
            bool(getattr(web_result, "company_verified", False)),
            bool(getattr(web_result, "person_verified", False)),
            bool(getattr(web_result, "industry_confirmed", False)),
            bool(getattr(web_result, "employee_count_confirmed", False)),
            bool(getattr(web_result, "technologies_found", None)),
            bool(growth and "none" not in str(growth).lower()),
        ]
        return round((sum(checks) / len(checks)) * 10, 1)
    except Exception:
        return 0.0


def _mark_lead_finished(db: Session, lead: Lead, failed: bool) -> None:
    """Idempotently finish a lead and advance its job in the same transaction."""
    if getattr(lead, "processing_status", "pending") in ("completed", "failed"):
        return
    from sqlalchemy import update
    lead.processing_status = "failed" if failed else "completed"
    values = {"processed_rows": Job.processed_rows + 1}
    if failed:
        values["failed_rows"] = Job.failed_rows + 1
    db.execute(update(Job).where(Job.id == lead.job_id).values(**values))
    db.flush()


def _get_cached_web(key: str):
    try:
        payload = _redis_cache.get(key)
        if payload:
            data = json.loads(payload)
            result = WebResearchResult(**data)
            result.raw_output = json.dumps(data, default=str)
            return result
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.debug("Redis web cache read unavailable: %s", exc)
    with _web_cache_lock:
        return _web_research_cache.get(key)


def _set_cached_web(key: str, result) -> None:
    try:
        payload = result.to_dict()
        _redis_cache.setex(key, 86400, json.dumps(payload, default=str))
        return
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.debug("Redis web cache write unavailable: %s", exc)
    with _web_cache_lock:
        # Bound fallback memory in workers when Redis cache commands fail.
        if len(_web_research_cache) >= 1000:
            _web_research_cache.clear()
        _web_research_cache[key] = result

"""
utils/column_mapper.py
──────────────────────
Dynamic, LLM-first column mapper for production use.

Strategy (in order):
  1. Exact match  — instant, zero cost, handles known CRM sources
  2. LLM mapping  — sends ALL unmatched column headers + up to 3 sample
                    data rows so the model can read actual cell values
                    before deciding the canonical mapping.
                    One batched call covers ALL unresolved columns.
  3. Fuzzy match  — difflib safety net applied AFTER LLM, only to correct
                    hallucinated output values (not as a mapping strategy)

Why sample rows matter:
  A column called "Field_47", "Info2", or "Exec_Detail" tells you nothing.
  But if the first three values are "mike@acme.com", "j.doe@corp.io",
  "sarah@techco.com" — it's obviously an email column.
  The LLM reads both the header AND the values before mapping.

Special tokens:
  _FIRST_NAME  → combine with _LAST_NAME into "Contact Name"
  _LAST_NAME   → combine with _FIRST_NAME into "Contact Name"
  _IGNORE      → drop this column entirely

Import pattern:
    from app.utils.column_mapper import map_columns, apply_mapping

    mapping = map_columns(df.columns.tolist(), sample_df=df)
    df_clean = apply_mapping(df, mapping)
"""

import json
import logging
import re
from difflib import get_close_matches
from typing import Optional

import pandas as pd

from app.config import (
    CANONICAL_COLUMNS,
    COLUMN_MAPPING,
    _FIRST_NAME_KEY,
    _LAST_NAME_KEY,
    _IGNORE_KEY,
)
# FIX #1: use a dedicated column-mapping system prompt, NOT the ICP validator's.
from app.llm_engine import llm_json, SYSTEM_COLUMN_MAPPER

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── Config ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SAMPLE_ROWS     = 3     # data rows sent to LLM per column
FUZZY_THRESHOLD = 0.82  # used only for post-LLM output correction
MAX_CELL_CHARS  = 120   # truncate long cell values before sending to LLM

# ── PII masking (applied to sample values BEFORE sending to the LLM) ──────────
# MASK_PII_SAMPLES  : mask emails + formatted phone numbers (safe, high value).
# MASK_NAMES        : also mask person-name-like tokens. OFF by default because
#                     names cannot be reliably distinguished from company/city/
#                     title values, so masking them can hurt mapping accuracy.
MASK_PII_SAMPLES = True
MASK_NAMES       = False

_EMAIL_RE        = re.compile(r"^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$")
# Phone: leading ( / digit / +, then digits + phone separators.
_PHONE_RE        = re.compile(r"^\+?[\d(][\d\s().\-]{5,}\d$")
# A phone MUST contain a formatting signal (+, space, -, (), .); this prevents a
# bare digit-run like "1500000" (revenue) or "94043" (zip) being masked.
_PHONE_FORMAT_RE = re.compile(r"[+\s().\-]")
_PHONE_KEEP_PREFIX = 4   # keep first 4 digits (country/area shape), mask the rest

_SOCIAL_DOMAINS = {
    "facebook.com", "twitter.com", "x.com", "linkedin.com",
    "instagram.com", "youtube.com", "tiktok.com", "t.co",
    "pinterest.com", "snapchat.com",
}

def _is_social_url(url: str) -> bool:
    """Return True if the URL belongs to a known social media domain."""
    url = url.strip().lower()
    return any(domain in url for domain in _SOCIAL_DOMAINS)

def _looks_like_phone(v: str) -> bool:
    if not _PHONE_RE.match(v):
        return False
    if sum(c.isdigit() for c in v) < 7:
        return False
    if not _PHONE_FORMAT_RE.search(v):   # plain integer → not a phone
        return False
    return True


def _mask_email(v: str) -> str:
    local, _, domain = v.partition("@")
    if not local:
        return v
    return f"{local[0]}{'*' * max(2, len(local) - 1)}@{domain}"


def _mask_phone(v: str) -> str:
    seen, out = 0, []
    for ch in v:
        if ch.isdigit():
            seen += 1
            out.append(ch if seen <= _PHONE_KEEP_PREFIX else "*")
        else:
            out.append(ch)   # keep +, spaces, -, (), .
    return "".join(out)


def _mask_name_token(tok: str) -> str:
    return tok if len(tok) <= 1 else tok[0] + "*" * (len(tok) - 1)


def _mask_pii(value: str) -> str:
    """
    Structure-preserving PII mask for a single sample value.

    - Emails  → m***@domain           (still obviously an email)
    - Phones  → +1 415 *** ****        (only if formatted; plain ints untouched)
    - Names   → C***** R******         (ONLY when MASK_NAMES is True)
    - Everything else (titles, companies, cities, revenue, ids) → untouched,
      so the LLM keeps the signal it needs to map the column correctly.
    """
    v = (value or "").strip()
    if not v:
        return v
    if _EMAIL_RE.match(v):
        return _mask_email(v)
    if _looks_like_phone(v):
        return _mask_phone(v)
    if MASK_NAMES:
        parts = v.split()
        if 1 <= len(parts) <= 4 and all(p[:1].isalpha() for p in parts):
            return " ".join(_mask_name_token(p) for p in parts)
    return v

# Pre-build O(1) exact lookup (lowercase stripped keys)
_EXACT_LOOKUP: dict[str, str] = {
    k.lower().strip(): v for k, v in COLUMN_MAPPING.items()
}

# Valid canonical output values the LLM is allowed to return
_VALID_OUTPUTS: set[str] = (
    set(CANONICAL_COLUMNS) | {_FIRST_NAME_KEY, _LAST_NAME_KEY, _IGNORE_KEY}
)


# ── Public API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def map_columns(input_columns, sample_df=None, job_id=None) -> dict[str, str]:
    """
    Map input column headers to canonical column names.

    Args:
        input_columns: raw column header strings from the uploaded file.
        sample_df:     the raw DataFrame — used to extract sample cell values.
                       Always pass this; without it the LLM works blind.

    Returns:
        {original_header: canonical_name | _FIRST_NAME | _LAST_NAME | _IGNORE}
    """
    result:    dict[str, str] = {}
    llm_queue: list[str]      = []

    for col in input_columns:
        canonical = _try_exact(col)
        if canonical:
            result[col] = canonical
            logger.debug(f"Exact match  '{col}' → '{canonical}'")
        else:
            llm_queue.append(col)

    if llm_queue:
        samples     = _extract_samples(llm_queue, sample_df)
        llm_results = _llm_map(llm_queue, samples, job_id=job_id)

        for col, mapped in llm_results.items():
            if mapped in _VALID_OUTPUTS:
                result[col] = mapped
            else:
                # LLM returned something not in our schema — try to fuzzy-correct
                corrected = _fuzzy_correct(mapped)
                if corrected:
                    logger.warning(f"LLM returned '{mapped}' for '{col}' → corrected to '{corrected}'")
                    result[col] = corrected
                else:
                    logger.warning(f"LLM returned unrecognised '{mapped}' for '{col}' → _IGNORE")
                    result[col] = _IGNORE_KEY

    return result


def apply_mapping(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mapping: dict[str, str],
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Apply a column mapping to a DataFrame.

    - Renames mapped columns to their canonical names.
    - Combines _FIRST_NAME + _LAST_NAME into "Contact Name".
    - Drops _IGNORE columns.
    - COALESCES columns that collide on the same canonical name (keep-first-
      NON-EMPTY per row) instead of blindly dropping duplicates — so real data
      in a second column isn't silently lost. (FIX #2)
    - Adds missing canonical columns as empty strings.
    - Reorders to CANONICAL_COLUMNS order.
    """
    first_name_col: Optional[str] = None
    last_name_col:  Optional[str] = None
    ignore_cols:    list[str]     = []

    # canonical_name → list of ORIGINAL source columns mapped to it
    targets: dict[str, list[str]] = {}

    for original, canonical in mapping.items():
        if original not in df.columns:
            continue
        if canonical == _IGNORE_KEY:
            ignore_cols.append(original)
        elif canonical == _FIRST_NAME_KEY:
            first_name_col = original
        elif canonical == _LAST_NAME_KEY:
            last_name_col = original
        else:
            targets.setdefault(canonical, []).append(original)

    out = pd.DataFrame(index=df.index)

    # ── Coalesce each canonical target from its source column(s) ──────────────
    for canonical, sources in targets.items():
        series = _coalesce_columns(df, sources, canonical=canonical)
        if len(sources) > 1:
            logger.warning(
                f"Column collision on '{canonical}': {sources} — "
                f"coalesced (first non-empty per row wins)"
            )
        out[canonical] = series

    # ── Combine first + last name → Contact Name ─────────────────────────────
    if first_name_col or last_name_col:
        fn = (
            df[first_name_col].fillna("").astype(str)
            if first_name_col and first_name_col in df.columns
            else pd.Series([""] * len(df), index=df.index)
        )
        ln = (
            df[last_name_col].fillna("").astype(str)
            if last_name_col and last_name_col in df.columns
            else pd.Series([""] * len(df), index=df.index)
        )
        combined = (fn.str.strip() + " " + ln.str.strip()).str.strip()

        if "Contact Name" not in out.columns:
            out["Contact Name"] = combined
        else:
            # A full-name column already mapped; fill only where it's empty.
            existing = out["Contact Name"].astype(str).str.strip()
            mask = existing == ""
            out.loc[mask, "Contact Name"] = combined[mask]

    # ignore_cols are simply never copied into `out` (implicitly dropped).

    # ── Add missing canonical columns as empty strings ───────────────────────
    for col in CANONICAL_COLUMNS:
        if col not in out.columns:
            out[col] = ""

    # ── Reorder to canonical order ───────────────────────────────────────────
    return out[CANONICAL_COLUMNS]


# ── Coalesce helper (keep-first-non-empty per row) ────────────────────────────
def _coalesce_columns(df: pd.DataFrame, sources: list[str],
                      canonical: str = "") -> pd.Series:
    """
    Merge source columns into one series — first non-empty per row wins.
    For Company Website: skip social media URLs entirely.
    """
    result = df[sources[0]].fillna("").astype(str).str.strip()
    for src in sources[1:]:
        col = df[src].fillna("").astype(str).str.strip()
        mask = result == ""
        result = result.where(~mask, col)

    # For Company Website — filter out any social URLs that slipped through
    if canonical == "Company Website":
        result = result.apply(
            lambda url: "" if _is_social_url(url) else url
        )

    return result


# ── LLM mapping ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _llm_map(columns, samples, job_id=None) -> dict[str, str]:
    """
    Single LLM call that inspects column names AND sample cell values.
    Returns {original_col: canonical_name_or_token}.
    """
    col_blocks = []
    for col in columns:
        vals = samples.get(col, [])
        vals_str = " | ".join(f'"{v}"' for v in vals) if vals else "(no data in file)"
        col_blocks.append(f'  Column: "{col}"\n  Sample values: {vals_str}')

    col_section = "\n\n".join(col_blocks)

    prompt = f"""You are a data engineer mapping columns from an unknown CRM or data export
to a canonical B2B lead schema. Determine the correct mapping by reading
BOTH the column header name AND the actual sample values from the file.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CANONICAL OUTPUT COLUMNS (use these exact strings as output values):
{json.dumps(CANONICAL_COLUMNS, indent=2)}

SPECIAL OUTPUT TOKENS:
  "_FIRST_NAME"  — column contains first name only (will be combined with last name)
  "_LAST_NAME"   — column contains last name only
  "_IGNORE"      — column has no place in the canonical schema; drop it

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COLUMNS TO MAP — header + sample cell values from the actual uploaded file:

{col_section}

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
RULES:
1. Sample values are the PRIMARY signal when the column name is ambiguous,
   generic, or in a foreign language.
   Examples:
     "Field_47"   + values ["mike@acme.com", "j@corp.io"]  → "Email Address"
     "Info_2"     + values ["+1 415 555 0101", "+44 20..."] → "Direct Phone Number"
     "Ejecutivo"  + values ["CEO", "CTO", "VP Engineering"] → "Job Title"
     "Nombre"     + values ["Carlos Ramirez", "Priya Patel"] → "Contact Name"

2. Handle any CRM source: ZoomInfo, Salesforce, HubSpot, Apollo, InsideView,
   EFX, Lusha, LinkedIn Sales Nav, Cognism, or any custom export.

3. Name splitting:
   - Column has first names only  → "_FIRST_NAME"
   - Column has last names only   → "_LAST_NAME"
   - Column has full names        → "Contact Name"
   - If both a full-name column AND separate first/last exist, map full-name → "_IGNORE"

4. ALWAYS map these to "_IGNORE" — no exceptions:
   - Parent company fields (parent employees, parent revenue, parent website, etc.)
   - Ultimate parent company fields
   - Internal CRM metadata (record IDs that aren't CRM Account/Contact ID)
   - Source-system quality/confidence scores (e.g. "Contact Quality Score",
     "Confidence Score", "Contact Accuracy Score" from the source system)
   - Social media follower counts, Alexa rank, internal codes
   - Social media profile URLs of ANY kind — Facebook, Twitter/X, Instagram,
     TikTok, YouTube, Blog URLs. These are NOT company websites.
     Examples: "Company Facebook Profile", "Company Twitter Profile",
     "Executive Twitter Profile", "Company Blog Profile" → ALL "_IGNORE"
   - "Company Website" maps ONLY to an actual corporate domain URL
     (e.g. https://www.acme.com). If sample values show facebook.com,
     twitter.com, linkedin.com, or any social domain → "_IGNORE"
   - Geographic area/territory/region labels for a person
     (e.g. "Executive Area", "Territory", "Sales Region") → "_IGNORE"
     These are NOT cities — only map to "Contact City" when values are
     clearly city names like "New York", "London", "Chennai"
   - Age, Salary, Education, Marital Status, personal demographics → "_IGNORE"
   - Fiscal year, quarter, SIC, NAICS, British SIC codes → "_IGNORE"
   - Employment status fields (Active, Terminated, etc.) → "_IGNORE"
   - Currency fields (Revenue Currency, Salary Currency) → "_IGNORE"
   - Count fields (Site Count, Location Count) → "_IGNORE"

5. Map to "CRM Account ID" if it looks like the source system's company identifier.
   Map to "CRM Contact ID" if it looks like the source system's person identifier.
   Examples: "EFX ID", "ZoomInfo Contact ID", "Salesforce Contact ID",
   "HubSpot Contact ID", "Apollo ID", "Executive ID" → "CRM Contact ID"

6. "Company Website" rule — STRICT:
   - Map to "Company Website" ONLY when sample values are real corporate URLs
     (e.g. https://www.acme.com, acme.io, acme.co.uk)
   - If the column is named "Website" but samples show social URLs → "_IGNORE"
   - NEVER map Facebook, Twitter, LinkedIn, Instagram, blog URLs to "Company Website"

7. Phone number disambiguation:
   - Direct/desk/office phone → "Direct Phone Number"
   - Mobile/cell phone → "Phone Number"
   - HQ/main company phone → "HQ Phone Number"
   - If unclear from name, use sample values to decide

8. If genuinely uncertain after reading both name and values → "_IGNORE"

9. Every input column key MUST appear in your response.
   Output values MUST be exactly one of the canonical strings or special tokens above.
   Do NOT invent new column names.

Return ONLY a valid JSON object — no markdown, no explanation:
{{
  "Column Header": "Canonical Column Name or _TOKEN",
  ...
}}"""
    
    try:
        # FIX #1: dedicated column-mapper system prompt (was SYSTEM_ICP_VALIDATOR)
        result = llm_json(prompt, system=SYSTEM_COLUMN_MAPPER,
                      stage="column_map", module="column_mapper",
                      job_id=job_id)

        mapped: dict[str, str] = {}
        for col in columns:
            val = result.get(col, _IGNORE_KEY)
            mapped[col] = val if val else _IGNORE_KEY
            logger.info(f"LLM mapped  '{col}' → '{mapped[col]}'")

        return mapped

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"LLM column mapping failed: {e} — ignoring {len(columns)} columns")
        return {col: _IGNORE_KEY for col in columns}


# ── Sample data extractor ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _extract_samples(
    columns: list[str],
    df: Optional[pd.DataFrame],
) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    """
    Extract up to SAMPLE_ROWS non-empty cell values per column.
    Truncates long values to MAX_CELL_CHARS to keep the prompt lean.
    """
    if df is None or df.empty:
        return {col: [] for col in columns}

    samples: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    for col in columns:
        if col not in df.columns:
            samples[col] = []
            continue

        col_series = df[col].dropna().astype(str).str.strip()
        # Filter out empty/null-like strings
        col_series = col_series[~col_series.isin(["", "nan", "None", "NaN", "none"])]
        vals = col_series.head(SAMPLE_ROWS).tolist()
        # Truncate first, then mask PII (email/phone) before it leaves the box.
        cleaned = [v[:MAX_CELL_CHARS] for v in vals]
        if MASK_PII_SAMPLES:
            cleaned = [_mask_pii(v) for v in cleaned]
        samples[col] = cleaned

    return samples


# ── Internal helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _try_exact(col: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """O(1) exact match against the static COLUMN_MAPPING dict."""
    return _EXACT_LOOKUP.get(col.lower().strip())


def _fuzzy_correct(llm_output: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """
    Post-LLM sanity check: if the LLM returned something close to a
    canonical column name, correct it. Never used as a primary mapping strategy.
    """
    lower_canonicals = [c.lower() for c in CANONICAL_COLUMNS]
    matches = get_close_matches(
        llm_output.lower().strip(),
        lower_canonicals,
        n=1,
        cutoff=FUZZY_THRESHOLD,
    )
    if matches:
        idx = lower_canonicals.index(matches[0])
        return CANONICAL_COLUMNS[idx]
    return None

END FILE: backend/app/utils/column_mapper.py

PATH: backend/app/utils/company_logger.py

"""
utils/company_logger.py
───────────────────────
Per-company file logger for DEEP pipeline debugging. (HARDENED edition)

Creates one log file per company under the root-level `Logs/` folder, capturing
the FULL raw output (JSON) of every pipeline stage for a single lead:

    Stage 1 ICP  →  Stage 2 Web  →  Website Scrape  →  AI Enrich
                 →  Stage 3 Contact  →  Scoring

New fields added anywhere in the pipeline (e.g. ICP `major_disqualifiers`) are
captured automatically, because stage results are serialised via to_jsonable()
which dumps the whole object.

Production hardening in this edition:
  • to_jsonable() has a RECURSION-DEPTH guard + cycle detection, so a circular
    reference can never cause a stack-overflow crash.
  • _dumps() caps output length so a giant LLM raw_output can't produce
    multi-MB log lines / runaway files.
  • Collections are capped; every path is exception-safe.
  • scrape() coerces a non-dict `research` safely.

Each stage also records the SOURCE .py FILE that produced the result. Output is
echoed to stdout AND written to a per-company file.
"""

import inspect
import json
import os
import re
import threading
import traceback
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone

# Root-level Logs directory  ( <project_root>/Logs )
LOGS_DIR = os.path.join(os.getcwd(), "Logs")

# India Standard Time (UTC+5:30) — fixed offset, no external tz library needed.
IST = timezone(timedelta(hours=5, minutes=30))

_dir_lock = threading.Lock()

# Serialisation safety limits.
_MAX_DEPTH        = 12       # recursion guard for to_jsonable
_MAX_ITEMS        = 500      # cap list/tuple/set length
_MAX_DICT_KEYS    = 500      # cap dict size
_MAX_DUMP_CHARS   = 200_000  # cap a single serialised block (~200 KB)


# ── Filesystem helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _ensure_logs_dir() -> str:
    try:
        with _dir_lock:
            os.makedirs(LOGS_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return LOGS_DIR


def _job_log_dir(job_id) -> str:
    """
    Return (creating if needed) the per-job folder that holds all company logs:
        Logs/<dd-mm-yyyy_HH-MM>_IST - <jobID>/
    ONE folder per job — reuse an existing folder ending with this job id so all
    companies land together even though leads run in parallel.
    """
    _ensure_logs_dir()
    jid = str(job_id) if job_id else "no-job"
    try:
        with _dir_lock:
            for name in os.listdir(LOGS_DIR):
                full = os.path.join(LOGS_DIR, name)
                if os.path.isdir(full) and name.endswith(f"- {jid}"):
                    return full
            stamp = datetime.now(IST).strftime("%d-%m-%Y_%H-%M")
            folder = os.path.join(LOGS_DIR, f"{stamp}_IST - {jid}")
            os.makedirs(folder, exist_ok=True)
            return folder
    except Exception:
        # Worst case, fall back to the root Logs dir so logging still works.
        return LOGS_DIR


def _sanitize(name) -> str:
    """Make a filesystem-safe file stem from a company name."""
    if not name or not str(name).strip():
        return "unknown_company"
    stem = str(name).strip()
    stem = re.sub(r"[^\w\-. ]+", "_", stem)   # drop odd chars -> _
    stem = re.sub(r"\s+", "_", stem)          # spaces -> _
    stem = stem.strip("._")
    return stem[:120] or "unknown_company"


# ── Source-file resolution ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def resolve_source(obj) -> str:
    """
    Figure out WHICH .py file produced a result, for traceability.
    Accepts a callable, a string (returned as-is), or a result object.
    Returns e.g. 'app/validators/icp_validator.py', or '-' if unknown.
    Never raises.
    """
    if obj is None:
        return "-"
    if isinstance(obj, str):
        return obj

    module = None
    try:
        if callable(obj) or inspect.ismodule(obj):
            module = inspect.getmodule(obj)
        else:
            module = inspect.getmodule(type(obj))
    except Exception:
        module = None

    try:
        src_file = inspect.getsourcefile(obj) if (callable(obj) or inspect.ismodule(obj)) \
            else inspect.getsourcefile(type(obj))
        if src_file:
            src_file = src_file.replace("\\", "/")
            marker = "/app/"
            if marker in src_file:
                return "app/" + src_file.split(marker, 1)[1]
            return os.path.basename(src_file)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if module is not None:
        try:
            name = getattr(module, "__name__", None)
            if name:
                return name.replace(".", "/") + ".py"
        except Exception:
            pass
    return "-"


# ── Serialisation (recursion + cycle safe) ────────────────────────────────────
def to_jsonable(obj, _depth: int = 0, _seen: set | None = None):
    """
    Best-effort conversion of ANY object to a JSON-serialisable structure.
    Handles pydantic v1/v2 models, dataclasses, ORM-ish objects, dicts, lists.

    Hardened:
      • depth-limited (prevents stack overflow on deeply nested / recursive data)
      • cycle-detected via object ids (prevents infinite loops)
      • collections capped in length
    """
    if obj is None or isinstance(obj, (str, int, float, bool)):
        return obj

    if _depth >= _MAX_DEPTH:
        return "…(max depth)"

    if _seen is None:
        _seen = set()
    oid = id(obj)
    # Only track containers/objects (not immutables) for cycle detection.
    if not isinstance(obj, (str, bytes, int, float, bool)):
        if oid in _seen:
            return "…(circular)"
        _seen = _seen | {oid}

    # Pydantic v2
    if hasattr(obj, "model_dump"):
        try:
            return to_jsonable(obj.model_dump(), _depth + 1, _seen)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Pydantic v1 / anything exposing .dict()
    dict_attr = getattr(obj, "dict", None)
    if callable(dict_attr):
        try:
            return to_jsonable(dict_attr(), _depth + 1, _seen)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Mapping
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        out = {}
        for i, (k, v) in enumerate(obj.items()):
            if i >= _MAX_DICT_KEYS:
                out["…"] = f"(+{len(obj) - _MAX_DICT_KEYS} more keys)"
                break
            try:
                out[str(k)] = to_jsonable(v, _depth + 1, _seen)
            except Exception:
                out[str(k)] = "…(unserialisable)"
        return out

    # Iterable
    if isinstance(obj, (list, tuple, set)):
        out = []
        for i, v in enumerate(obj):
            if i >= _MAX_ITEMS:
                out.append(f"…(+{len(obj) - _MAX_ITEMS} more items)")
                break
            try:
                out.append(to_jsonable(v, _depth + 1, _seen))
            except Exception:
                out.append("…(unserialisable)")
        return out

    # Generic object with attributes
    if hasattr(obj, "__dict__"):
        out = {}
        try:
            for k, v in vars(obj).items():
                if k.startswith("_"):
                    continue
                try:
                    out[k] = to_jsonable(v, _depth + 1, _seen)
                except Exception:
                    out[k] = "…(unserialisable)"
        except Exception:
            return str(obj)
        return out

    try:
        return str(obj)
    except Exception:
        return "…(unserialisable)"


def _dumps(obj) -> str:
    """Serialise to pretty JSON, size-capped so it can't bloat the log."""
    try:
        text = json.dumps(to_jsonable(obj), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)
    except Exception as e:
        try:
            text = f"<unserialisable: {e}> {obj!r}"
        except Exception:
            text = "<unserialisable>"
    if len(text) > _MAX_DUMP_CHARS:
        return text[:_MAX_DUMP_CHARS] + f"\n…(truncated, {len(text)} chars total)"
    return text


# ── The logger ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
class CompanyLogger:
    """
    One instance == one company log file. Thread-safe writes so it plays nicely
    with Celery's threads worker pool. Every public method is exception-safe.
    """

    _write_lock = threading.Lock()

    def __init__(
        self,
        company_name,
        contact_name=None,
        lead_id=None,
        job_id=None,
        icp_prompt=None,
    ) -> None:
        self.company_name = company_name or "Unknown Company"
        self.contact_name = contact_name
        self.lead_id = lead_id
        self.job_id = job_id
        self.icp_prompt = icp_prompt
        self._t0 = datetime.now(IST)

        try:
            job_dir = _job_log_dir(job_id)
            stem = _sanitize(company_name)
            suffix = f"_{str(lead_id)[:8]}" if lead_id else ""
            self.path = os.path.join(job_dir, f"{stem}{suffix}.log")
        except Exception:
            # Fall back to a safe path so logging never crashes construction.
            self.path = os.path.join(_ensure_logs_dir(), "fallback.log")

    # ── low-level write ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    def _emit(self, text) -> None:
        try:
            text = text if isinstance(text, str) else str(text)
        except Exception:
            text = "<unprintable>"
        try:
            print(text, flush=True)                    # live console
        except Exception:
            pass
        try:
            with self._write_lock:
                with open(self.path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                    f.write(text + "\n")
        except Exception as e:
            try:
                print(f"[CompanyLogger] failed to write {self.path}: {e}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass

    def _rule(self, char: str = "─") -> None:
        self._emit(char * 80)

    # ── public API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    def header(self, lead_data=None) -> None:
        try:
            self._emit("=" * 80)
            self._emit("LEAD PROCESSING LOG")
            self._emit(f"Company     : {self.company_name}")
            self._emit(f"Contact     : {self.contact_name or '-'}")
            self._emit(f"Lead ID     : {self.lead_id}")
            self._emit(f"Job ID      : {self.job_id}")
            self._emit(f"Started     : {self._t0.isoformat()}")
            if self.icp_prompt:
                self._emit(f"ICP Prompt  : {self.icp_prompt}")
            self._emit("=" * 80)
            if lead_data is not None:
                self._emit("")
                self._emit(">>> INPUT LEAD DATA (raw):")
                self._emit(_dumps(lead_data))
        except Exception as e:
            self._emit(f"[header error: {e}]")

    def stage(self, title, result, source=None, inputs=None, extra=None, started=None) -> None:
        """Log one pipeline stage with its full raw JSON output. Never raises."""
        try:
            self._emit("")
            self._rule()
            self._emit(str(title))
            self._emit(f"validated by : {resolve_source(source if source is not None else result)}")
            self._rule()

            if inputs is not None:
                self._emit(">>> INPUT:")
                self._emit(_dumps(inputs))

            self._emit(">>> RAW OUTPUT (json):")
            self._emit(_dumps(result))

            raw = getattr(result, "raw_output", None)
            if raw:
                self._emit(">>> RAW LLM OUTPUT:")
                self._emit(_dumps(raw) if not isinstance(raw, str) else
                           (raw[:_MAX_DUMP_CHARS] +
                            (f"\n…(truncated, {len(raw)} chars total)" if len(raw) > _MAX_DUMP_CHARS else "")))

            if extra:
                for label, value in (extra.items() if isinstance(extra, dict) else []):
                    self._emit(f">>> {label}:")
                    self._emit(_dumps(value))

            if started:
                self._emit(f">>> duration: {_duration(started):.2f}s")
        except Exception as e:
            self._emit(f"[stage '{title}' error: {e}]")

    # ── Website scrape (RAG ingestion) ────────────────────────────────────────
    def scrape(
        self,
        research,
        clean_text: str | None = None,
        source=None,
        chunk_count: int | None = None,
        blob_path: str | None = None,
        max_chars: int = 4000,
        started=None,
    ) -> None:
        """Log the WEBSITE SCRAPE + PARSE step. Never raises."""
        try:
            research = research if isinstance(research, dict) else {}
            scraped_text = research.get("text", "") or ""
            if not isinstance(scraped_text, str):
                scraped_text = str(scraped_text)
            source_url = research.get("source_url", "") or ""
            src_kind   = research.get("source", "none")
            reachable  = research.get("reachable", False)

            body_for_len = clean_text if clean_text is not None else scraped_text
            if not isinstance(body_for_len, str):
                body_for_len = str(body_for_len)

            self._emit("")
            self._rule()
            self._emit("WEBSITE SCRAPE (RAG ingestion)")
            self._emit(f"scraped by : {resolve_source(source) if source else 'app/scraper/web_scraper.py'}")
            self._rule()

            self._emit(">>> SCRAPE SUMMARY:")
            self._emit(_dumps({
                "company":       self.company_name,
                "website_url":   source_url,
                "source":        src_kind,
                "reachable":     reachable,
                "scraped_chars": len(scraped_text),
                "merged_chars":  len(body_for_len),
                "chunk_count":   chunk_count if chunk_count is not None else "(pending)",
                "blob_path":     blob_path or "-",
                "will_embed":    bool(body_for_len.strip()),
            }))

            self._emit(">>> SCRAPED TEXT (raw" +
                       (f", first {max_chars} of {len(scraped_text)} chars"
                        if len(scraped_text) > max_chars else f", {len(scraped_text)} chars") + "):")
            if scraped_text:
                self._emit(scraped_text[:max_chars] +
                           ("\n… [truncated]" if len(scraped_text) > max_chars else ""))
            else:
                self._emit("∅ (no text scraped — site unreachable or empty)")

            if clean_text is not None and clean_text != scraped_text:
                ct = clean_text if isinstance(clean_text, str) else str(clean_text)
                self._emit(">>> MERGED TEXT TO EMBED (scrape + enrichment, first "
                           f"{max_chars} of {len(ct)} chars):")
                self._emit(ct[:max_chars] + ("\n… [truncated]" if len(ct) > max_chars else ""))

            if started:
                self._emit(f">>> duration: {_duration(started):.2f}s")
        except Exception as e:
            self._emit(f"[scrape log error: {e}]")

    def note(self, message) -> None:
        self._emit(str(message))

    def error(self, message, exc: Exception | None = None) -> None:
        try:
            self._emit("")
            self._rule("!")
            self._emit(f"ERROR: {message}")
            if exc:
                try:
                    self._emit(traceback.format_exc())
                except Exception:
                    self._emit(f"(exc: {exc})")
            self._rule("!")
        except Exception:
            pass

    def close(self, score_result=None) -> None:
        try:
            total = _duration(self._t0)
            self._emit("")
            self._rule("=")
            if score_result is not None:
                self._emit(">>> FINAL SCORE (json):")
                self._emit(f"scored by : {resolve_source(score_result)}")
                self._emit(_dumps(score_result))
            self._emit(f">>> TOTAL PIPELINE DURATION: {total:.2f}s")
            self._emit(f">>> LOG FILE: {self.path}")
            self._rule("=")
        except Exception as e:
            self._emit(f"[close error: {e}]")


# ── Duration helper (timezone-aware safe) ─────────────────────────────────────
def _duration(started) -> float:
    """
    Seconds elapsed since `started`. Works whether `started` is UTC or IST
    (Python subtracts tz-aware datetimes correctly). Never raises.
    """
    try:
        now = datetime.now(started.tzinfo) if getattr(started, "tzinfo", None) else datetime.now(IST)
        return (now - started).total_seconds()
    except Exception:
        return 0.0

END FILE: backend/app/utils/company_logger.py

"""
utils/deduplicator.py
──────────────────────
Removes duplicate rows from the parsed DataFrame before processing.

Deduplication strategy (in order, first match wins):
  1. Exact CRM Contact ID match (crm_contact_id) — strongest signal
  2. Exact email address match (case-insensitive)
  3. Normalized exact name + company match — catches casing/punctuation variants

The first occurrence of each duplicate group is kept; subsequent ones
are dropped and logged.

Import pattern:
    from app.utils.deduplicator import deduplicate

    df, stats = deduplicate(df)
    # stats: {"original": int, "duplicates_removed": int, "final": int}
"""

import logging
import pandas as pd
import re

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

def deduplicate(df: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict[str, int]]:
    """
    Remove duplicate lead rows from a 29-column DataFrame.

    Returns:
        (deduplicated_df, stats_dict)
        stats_dict has keys: original, duplicates_removed, final
    """
    original_count = len(df)
    keep = [True] * len(df)   # True = keep this row

    # ── Pass 1: CRM Contact ID ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    if "CRM Contact ID" in df.columns:
        seen_ids: set[str] = set()
        for i, val in enumerate(df["CRM Contact ID"]):
            v = str(val).strip().lower()
            if v and v not in ("", "nan", "none"):
                if v in seen_ids:
                    keep[i] = False
                else:
                    seen_ids.add(v)

    # ── Pass 2: Email address ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    if "Email Address" in df.columns:
        seen_emails: set[str] = set()
        for i, val in enumerate(df["Email Address"]):
            if not keep[i]:
                continue
            v = str(val).strip().lower()
            if v and v not in ("", "nan", "none"):
                if v in seen_emails:
                    keep[i] = False
                else:
                    seen_emails.add(v)

    # ── Pass 3: normalized exact name + company ──────────────────────────────
    name_col    = "Contact Name"    if "Contact Name"    in df.columns else None
    company_col = "Company Name"    if "Company Name"    in df.columns else None

    if name_col and company_col:
        seen_people: set[str] = set()

        for i in range(len(df)):
            if not keep[i]:
                continue

            name    = str(df.iloc[i][name_col]).strip().lower()
            company = str(df.iloc[i][company_col]).strip().lower()

            if not name and not company:
                continue

            norm_name = _normalise(name)
            norm_company = _normalise(company)
            signature = f"{norm_name}|{norm_company}"
            if signature in seen_people:
                keep[i] = False
            else:
                seen_people.add(signature)

    # ── Apply mask ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    clean_df = df[keep].reset_index(drop=True)
    removed  = original_count - len(clean_df)

    if removed > 0:
        logger.info(f"Deduplicator: removed {removed} duplicates ({original_count} → {len(clean_df)} rows)")

    stats = {
        "original":           original_count,
        "duplicates_removed": removed,
        "final":              len(clean_df),
    }
    return clean_df, stats


def _normalise(value: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", value.lower())

END FILE: backend/app/utils/deduplicator.py

PATH: backend/app/utils/logger.py

"""
utils/logger.py
───────────────
Thin wrapper that writes structured entries to the processing_logs table
AND emits to the standard Python logger simultaneously. (HARDENED edition)

Design principle: logging must NEVER crash the pipeline. Every message is
coerced to a safe non-empty string; DB write failures are swallowed; a missing
DB session degrades to console-only logging.

Import pattern everywhere in the pipeline:
    from app.utils.logger import pipeline_logger

    log = pipeline_logger(db, job_id="...", lead_id="...")
    log.info("Stage 1 passed")
    log.warning("Missing phone number")
    log.error("LLM call failed", exc=e)

One-time setup (call once at worker/app startup):
    from app.utils.logger import configure_logging
    configure_logging()
"""

import logging
import traceback
from typing import Optional

from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from app.models import ProcessingLog

# Standard Python logger (goes to stdout / your log aggregator)
_py_logger = logging.getLogger("ailead.pipeline")

# Cap DB messages so a runaway payload can't bloat the table / hit column limits.
MAX_DB_MESSAGE_CHARS = 20000


# ── Safe string coercion ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _safe_str(message, default: str = "(empty log message)") -> str:
    """
    Coerce ANY message to a non-empty string. Fixes the NOT-NULL crash when a
    None or non-string message reaches ProcessingLog(message=...).
    """
    if message is None:
        return default
    if isinstance(message, str):
        return message if message.strip() else default
    try:
        s = str(message)
        return s if s.strip() else default
    except Exception:
        return default


# ── One-time logging setup ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_CONFIGURED = False


def configure_logging(level: int = logging.INFO) -> None:
    """
    Configure root + third-party loggers for clean pipeline output.
    Idempotent — safe to call more than once. Never raises.
    """
    global _CONFIGURED
    if _CONFIGURED:
        return
    try:
        logging.basicConfig(
            level=level,
            format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(message)s",
            datefmt="%H:%M:%S",
        )
        # Silence libraries that dump raw HTTP URLs on every LLM call.
        for noisy in ("httpx", "httpcore", "openai", "urllib3", "azure", "azure.core"):
            logging.getLogger(noisy).setLevel(logging.WARNING)
        _py_logger.setLevel(level)
    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
        # Logging setup must never take down startup.
        logging.getLogger(__name__).warning(f"configure_logging failed: {e}")
    finally:
        _CONFIGURED = True


class PipelineLogger:
    """
    Thin dual-writer: Azure SQL processing_logs + Python logging.
    Instantiate once per task with the IDs you know at that point.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        db: Optional[Session],
        job_id: Optional[str] = None,
        lead_id: Optional[str] = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._db = db
        self._job_id = job_id
        self._lead_id = lead_id

    # ── Public methods ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    def info(self, message: str) -> None:
        msg = _safe_str(message)
        self._write("INFO", msg)
        _py_logger.info(self._fmt(msg))

    def warning(self, message: str) -> None:
        msg = _safe_str(message)
        self._write("WARNING", msg)
        _py_logger.warning(self._fmt(msg))

    def error(self, message: str, exc: Optional[Exception] = None) -> None:
        # DB entry gets the full traceback; console stays clean (one-line reason).
        msg = _safe_str(message)
        db_message = msg
        console_message = msg

        if exc is not None:
            try:
                tb = traceback.format_exc()
            except Exception:
                tb = ""
            if tb and tb.strip() and tb.strip() != "NoneType: None":
                db_message = f"{msg}\n{tb}"
            console_message = f"{msg}: {exc}"

        self._write("ERROR", db_message)
        _py_logger.error(self._fmt(console_message))

    # Allow updating IDs mid-task (e.g. after the lead row is inserted)
    def set_lead_id(self, lead_id: str) -> None:
        self._lead_id = lead_id

    def set_job_id(self, job_id: str) -> None:
        self._job_id = job_id

    # ── Internal ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    def _write(self, level: str, message: str) -> None:
        # No DB session → console-only (still logged by the public methods).
        if self._db is None:
            return
        try:
            entry = ProcessingLog(
                job_id=self._job_id,
                lead_id=self._lead_id,
                level=level,
                message=_safe_str(message)[:MAX_DB_MESSAGE_CHARS],
            )
            self._db.add(entry)
            self._db.flush()   # write immediately without closing the transaction
        except Exception as e:
            # Never let logging blow up the pipeline.
            try:
                _py_logger.error(f"Failed to write processing_log to DB: {e}")
            except Exception:
                pass

    def _fmt(self, message: str) -> str:
        parts = []
        if self._job_id:
            parts.append(f"job={str(self._job_id)[:8]}")
        if self._lead_id:
            parts.append(f"lead={str(self._lead_id)[:8]}")
        prefix = f"[{' '.join(parts)}] " if parts else ""
        return f"{prefix}{message}"


# ── Factory function (preferred entry point) ──────────────────────────────────
def pipeline_logger(
    db: Optional[Session],
    job_id: Optional[str] = None,
    lead_id: Optional[str] = None,
) -> PipelineLogger:
    """
    Create a PipelineLogger bound to a DB session and optional IDs.

    Usage:
        with db_session() as db:
            log = pipeline_logger(db, job_id=job.id)
            log.info("Processing started")
    """
    return PipelineLogger(db=db, job_id=job_id, lead_id=lead_id)

END FILE: backend/app/utils/logger.py

PATH: backend/app/utils/monitor.py

"""
utils/monitor.py
────────────────
Centralised issue monitor — ONE place to see every WARNING, ERROR, and
RATE-LIMIT across ALL workers, instead of hunting through 500 per-company logs.

It writes to a small set of root-level files under  Logs/_monitor/ :

    Logs/_monitor/monitor_<dd-mm-yyyy>_IST.log     ← all WARNING + ERROR + rate-limit
    Logs/_monitor/rate_limits_<dd-mm-yyyy>_IST.log ← ONLY rate-limit (429) events

Every line is structured and tells you exactly WHO and WHERE:

  13:22:06 IST | RATE_LIMIT | worker=Thread-7 host=DESKTOP | job=3f9a2b7c |
      lead=1a2b3c4d | company=Acme Corp | src=app/validators/web_researcher.py:142 |
      429 Too Many Requests — retry after 30s

HOW IT WORKS
────────────
1. configure_monitor() attaches ONE logging handler to the root logger that
   captures every record at WARNING level or above — from your code AND from
   libraries (openai, httpx, azure). Rate-limit lines are auto-detected and
   also copied to the rate-limit file.
2. bind_context(job_id=..., lead_id=..., company=...) stamps each log line with
   the lead being processed, so you know which worker/lead hit the issue.

WIRE-IN
───────
  # once at startup (e.g. in celery_app.py, after configure_logging):
  from app.utils.monitor import configure_monitor
  configure_monitor()

  # at the top of process_lead(), after you know the ids:
  from app.utils.monitor import bind_context
  bind_context(job_id=lead.job_id, lead_id=lead_id, company=lead.company_name)

  # (optional) explicit rate-limit logging where you catch a 429:
  from app.utils.monitor import log_rate_limit
  log_rate_limit("web_researcher", "429 from Azure OpenAI", retry_after=30)
"""

import logging
import os
import re
import socket
import threading
from contextvars import ContextVar
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from logging.handlers import RotatingFileHandler

# ── Paths + time ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
MONITOR_DIR = os.path.join(os.getcwd(), "Logs", "_monitor")
IST = timezone(timedelta(hours=5, minutes=30))
_HOST = socket.gethostname()[:20]

# Rotate each file at ~10 MB, keep 5 backups → never unbounded.
_MAX_BYTES = 10 * 1024 * 1024
_BACKUPS = 5

# Patterns that mark a record as a rate-limit event.
_RATE_LIMIT_RE = re.compile(
    r"(429|rate[\s_-]?limit|ratelimit|too many requests|"
    r"requests? per minute|tokens? per minute|\bRPM\b|\bTPM\b|retry[- ]after)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)

# ── Per-task context (works across the threads worker pool) ───────────────────
_ctx_job:     ContextVar[str] = ContextVar("mon_job",     default="-")
_ctx_lead:    ContextVar[str] = ContextVar("mon_lead",    default="-")
_ctx_company: ContextVar[str] = ContextVar("mon_company", default="-")


def bind_context(job_id=None, lead_id=None, company=None) -> None:
    """Stamp subsequent log lines (this task/thread) with these identifiers."""
    if job_id is not None:
        _ctx_job.set(_short(job_id, 8))
    if lead_id is not None:
        _ctx_lead.set(_short(lead_id, 8))
    if company is not None:
        _ctx_company.set(_clip(str(company), 40))


def clear_context() -> None:
    _ctx_job.set("-"); _ctx_lead.set("-"); _ctx_company.set("-")


def _short(v, n):  # noqa
    s = str(v)
    return s[:n] if s else "-"


def _clip(s, n):
    return s if len(s) <= n else s[: n - 1] + "…"


# ── Formatter: structured, IST, with worker + source ──────────────────────────
class _MonitorFormatter(logging.Formatter):
    def format(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> str:
        ts = datetime.now(IST).strftime("%d-%m %H:%M:%S")
        worker = threading.current_thread().name[:14]
        # source file trimmed to the app/ package if possible
        src = record.pathname.replace("\\", "/")
        if "/app/" in src:
            src = "app/" + src.split("/app/", 1)[1]
        else:
            src = os.path.basename(src)
        src = f"{src}:{record.lineno}"

        kind = "RATE_LIMIT" if _is_rate_limit(record) else record.levelname
        msg = record.getMessage()

        # Append the exception reason (type + message) so the monitor shows WHY,
        # not just the log message — crucial when a rate-limit is raised, not typed.
        if record.exc_info:
            try:
                exc = record.exc_info[1]
                if exc is not None:
                    msg += f" | exc: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            except Exception:
                pass

        # Collapse newlines/tracebacks to keep the monitor scannable (one line).
        msg = _clip(msg.replace("\n", " ⏎ ").strip(), 500)

        return (
            f"{ts} IST | {kind:<10} | worker={worker} host={_HOST} | "
            f"job={_ctx_job.get()} | lead={_ctx_lead.get()} | "
            f"company={_ctx_company.get()} | src={src} | logger={record.name} | {msg}"
        )


def _is_rate_limit(record: logging.LogRecord) -> bool:
    try:
        text = record.getMessage()
    except Exception:
        text = str(getattr(record, "msg", ""))
    # Also inspect exception text if present.
    if record.exc_info:
        try:
            text += " " + str(record.exc_info[1])
        except Exception:
            pass
    return bool(_RATE_LIMIT_RE.search(text or ""))


# ── Filters ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
class _WarnAndAbove(logging.Filter):
    """Only let WARNING/ERROR/CRITICAL through to the monitor."""
    def filter(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> bool:
        return record.levelno >= logging.WARNING


class _RateLimitOnly(logging.Filter):
    def filter(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> bool:
        return _is_rate_limit(record)


# ── One-time setup ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_CONFIGURED = False
_lock = threading.Lock()


def configure_monitor(level: int = logging.WARNING) -> None:
    """
    Attach the central monitor to the root logger. Idempotent + crash-safe.
    Captures WARNING+ from ALL loggers (your code + openai/httpx/azure).
    """
    global _CONFIGURED
    with _lock:
        if _CONFIGURED:
            return
        try:
            os.makedirs(MONITOR_DIR, exist_ok=True)
            stamp = datetime.now(IST).strftime("%d-%m-%Y")
            fmt = _MonitorFormatter()

            # 1) Combined issues file (WARNING + ERROR + rate-limit).
            issues = RotatingFileHandler(
                os.path.join(MONITOR_DIR, f"monitor_{stamp}_IST.log"),
                maxBytes=_MAX_BYTES, backupCount=_BACKUPS, encoding="utf-8",
            )
            issues.setLevel(level)
            issues.addFilter(_WarnAndAbove())
            issues.setFormatter(fmt)

            # 2) Rate-limit-only file.
            ratelimits = RotatingFileHandler(
                os.path.join(MONITOR_DIR, f"rate_limits_{stamp}_IST.log"),
                maxBytes=_MAX_BYTES, backupCount=_BACKUPS, encoding="utf-8",
            )
            ratelimits.setLevel(logging.WARNING)
            ratelimits.addFilter(_RateLimitOnly())
            ratelimits.setFormatter(fmt)

            root = logging.getLogger()
            root.addHandler(issues)
            root.addHandler(ratelimits)
            # Ensure root passes WARNING+ through to our handlers.
            if root.level == logging.NOTSET or root.level > logging.WARNING:
                root.setLevel(logging.WARNING)

            _CONFIGURED = True
            logging.getLogger("ailead.monitor").info("central monitor configured")
        except Exception as e:  # never let monitor setup crash startup
            logging.getLogger(__name__).warning(f"configure_monitor failed: {e}")
            _CONFIGURED = True


# ── Explicit helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_mon = logging.getLogger("ailead.monitor")


def log_rate_limit(module: str, detail: str, retry_after=None) -> None:
    """Explicitly record a rate-limit event with rich context."""
    ra = f" | retry_after={retry_after}s" if retry_after is not None else ""
    _mon.warning(f"RATE LIMIT in {module}: {detail}{ra}")


def log_warning(module: str, detail: str) -> None:
    _mon.warning(f"{module}: {detail}")


def log_error(module: str, detail: str, exc: Exception | None = None) -> None:
    if exc is not None:
        _mon.error(f"{module}: {detail}: {exc}")
    else:
        _mon.error(f"{module}: {detail}")


def is_rate_limit_error(exc: Exception) -> bool:
    """True if an exception looks like an Azure/OpenAI rate-limit (429)."""
    try:
        if getattr(exc, "status_code", None) == 429:
            return True
        return bool(_RATE_LIMIT_RE.search(f"{type(exc).__name__} {exc}"))
    except Exception:
        return False

END FILE: backend/app/utils/monitor.py
