"""
validators/contact_verifier.py
───────────────────────────────
Stage 3 — Contact Quality Check.  (HARDENED, production-grade edition)

Three independent, best-effort checks per lead:
  1. Email      — syntax + DNS MX lookup (+ optional SMTP RCPT handshake)
  2. Phone      — parse/validate via `phonenumbers`
  3. Employment — from Stage 2 web research result

Design principles:
  • NEVER crash the Celery task — every check is isolated + guarded.
  • NEVER hang — DNS and SMTP have hard timeouts.
  • Cloud-aware scoring — many hosts (incl. Azure) BLOCK outbound port 25, so
    the SMTP deliverability probe reliably fails there. SMTP is therefore
    OPT-IN (settings.ENABLE_SMTP_CHECK, default off), and the quality score
    ADAPTS so a lead isn't silently penalised for an impossible check.

Import:
    from app.validators.contact_verifier import verify_contact
    result = verify_contact(lead_data, web_result)
"""

import json
import logging
import re
import smtplib
import socket
from dataclasses import dataclass

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── Optional deps guarded so an import failure can't kill the pipeline ─────────
try:
    import dns.resolver
    import dns.exception
    _DNS_OK = True
except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
    _DNS_OK = False
    logger.warning(f"dnspython unavailable — MX checks disabled: {e}")

try:
    import phonenumbers
    from phonenumbers import carrier, geocoder, PhoneNumberFormat
    _PHONE_OK = True
except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
    _PHONE_OK = False
    logger.warning(f"phonenumbers unavailable — phone checks disabled: {e}")

# ── Config (with safe fallbacks if settings lacks the keys) ───────────────────
try:
    from app.config import settings
    _ENABLE_SMTP  = bool(getattr(settings, "ENABLE_SMTP_CHECK", False))
    _SMTP_TIMEOUT = float(getattr(settings, "SMTP_TIMEOUT_S", 5))
    _DNS_TIMEOUT  = float(getattr(settings, "DNS_TIMEOUT_S", 5))
except Exception:
    _ENABLE_SMTP, _SMTP_TIMEOUT, _DNS_TIMEOUT = False, 5.0, 5.0

_SMTP_FROM_ADDR = "verify@ailead-verify.local"
_EMAIL_RE = re.compile(r"^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$")


# ── Safe helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _s(value, default: str = "") -> str:
    """None-safe stripped string."""
    if value is None:
        return default
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    try:
        return str(value).strip()
    except Exception:
        return default


# ── Result dataclass ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@dataclass
class ContactVerifyResult:
    email_valid: bool = False
    email_deliverable: bool = False
    email_notes: str = ""
    phone_valid: bool = False
    phone_e164: str = ""
    phone_carrier: str = ""
    phone_country: str = ""
    employment_confirmed: bool = False
    contact_quality_score: float = 0.0
    raw_output: str = ""

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "email_valid":           self.email_valid,
            "email_deliverable":     self.email_deliverable,
            "email_notes":           self.email_notes,
            "phone_valid":           self.phone_valid,
            "phone_e164":            self.phone_e164,
            "phone_carrier":         self.phone_carrier,
            "phone_country":         self.phone_country,
            "employment_confirmed":  self.employment_confirmed,
            "contact_quality_score": self.contact_quality_score,
        }


# ── Public API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def verify_contact(lead_data: dict, web_result=None) -> ContactVerifyResult:
    """
    Run Stage 3 contact verification. Never raises — each check is isolated.
    """
    result = ContactVerifyResult()

    if not isinstance(lead_data, dict):
        logger.warning("verify_contact: lead_data is not a dict — skipping")
        result.email_notes = "Invalid lead data"
        result.raw_output = json.dumps(result.to_dict())
        return result

    # ── 1. Email ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    email = _s(lead_data.get("Email Address"))
    if email:
        try:
            result.email_valid, result.email_deliverable, result.email_notes = _check_email(email)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"email check failed for '{email}': {e}")
            result.email_notes = f"Email check error: {e}"
    else:
        result.email_notes = "No email address provided"

    # ── 2. Phone ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    phone_raw = _s(lead_data.get("Direct Phone Number")) or _s(lead_data.get("Phone Number"))
    country_hint = _s(lead_data.get("Contact Country")) or "US"
    if phone_raw:
        try:
            (result.phone_valid, result.phone_e164,
             result.phone_carrier, result.phone_country) = _check_phone(phone_raw, country_hint)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"phone check failed for '{phone_raw}': {e}")

    # ── 3. Employment cross-check ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    if web_result is not None:
        try:
            result.employment_confirmed = bool(getattr(web_result, "person_verified", False))
        except Exception:
            result.employment_confirmed = False

    # ── 4. Composite quality score (adaptive) ─────────────────────────────────
    result.contact_quality_score = _score(result, email_present=bool(email))

    try:
        result.raw_output = json.dumps(result.to_dict(), default=str)
    except Exception:
        result.raw_output = "{}"

    logger.debug(
        f"Contact verify [{_s(lead_data.get('Contact Name')) or '?'}] "
        f"email_valid={result.email_valid} deliverable={result.email_deliverable} "
        f"phone={result.phone_valid} employment={result.employment_confirmed} "
        f"quality={result.contact_quality_score}"
    )
    return result


# ── Email check ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _check_email(email: str) -> tuple[bool, bool, str]:
    """
    Returns (email_valid, email_deliverable, notes).
      email_valid       = syntactically valid AND domain has an MX record.
      email_deliverable = SMTP RCPT accepted (ONLY attempted if enabled).
    """
    if not _EMAIL_RE.match(email):
        return False, False, "Invalid email syntax"

    domain = email.split("@", 1)[1]

    if not _DNS_OK:
        # Can't verify MX → treat as syntactically-valid only (don't over-claim).
        return True, False, "Syntax valid (MX check unavailable)"

    # DNS MX lookup with a hard timeout so it can never hang the task.
    try:
        resolver = dns.resolver.Resolver()
        resolver.timeout = _DNS_TIMEOUT
        resolver.lifetime = _DNS_TIMEOUT
        mx_records = resolver.resolve(domain, "MX")
        mx_host = str(sorted(mx_records, key=lambda r: r.preference)[0].exchange).rstrip(".")
    except Exception as e:
        return False, False, f"No MX record for '{domain}': {type(e).__name__}"

    # SMTP deliverability — opt-in only (port 25 is blocked on many cloud hosts).
    if not _ENABLE_SMTP:
        return True, False, f"MX: {mx_host} (SMTP probe disabled)"

    deliverable = _smtp_check(email, mx_host)
    notes = f"MX: {mx_host}" + ("" if deliverable else " | SMTP: rejected/timeout/blocked")
    return True, deliverable, notes


def _smtp_check(email: str, mx_host: str) -> bool:
    """RCPT TO probe (no mail sent). Never raises — any error → False."""
    try:
        with smtplib.SMTP(mx_host, 25, timeout=_SMTP_TIMEOUT) as smtp:
            smtp.ehlo_or_helo_if_needed()
            smtp.mail(_SMTP_FROM_ADDR)
            code, _ = smtp.rcpt(email)
            return code in (250, 251)
    except (smtplib.SMTPException, socket.timeout, OSError):
        return False


# ── Phone check ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _check_phone(raw: str, country_hint: str = "US") -> tuple[bool, str, str, str]:
    """Returns (phone_valid, e164, carrier_name, country_name)."""
    if not _PHONE_OK:
        return False, "", "", ""

    region = _country_to_iso(country_hint)
    try:
        parsed = phonenumbers.parse(raw, region)
    except Exception:
        # Retry once with no region in case the number is already E.164 (+…).
        try:
            parsed = phonenumbers.parse(raw, None)
        except Exception:
            return False, "", "", ""

    try:
        if not phonenumbers.is_valid_number(parsed):
            return False, "", "", ""
        e164  = phonenumbers.format_number(parsed, PhoneNumberFormat.E164)
        cname = carrier.name_for_number(parsed, "en") or ""
        cty   = geocoder.country_name_for_number(parsed, "en") or ""
        return True, e164, cname, cty
    except Exception:
        return False, "", "", ""


def _country_to_iso(country: str) -> str:
    _MAP = {
        "UNITED STATES": "US", "UNITED STATES OF AMERICA": "US", "USA": "US", "US": "US",
        "UNITED KINGDOM": "GB", "UK": "GB", "GB": "GB", "ENGLAND": "GB",
        "CANADA": "CA", "CA": "CA",
        "AUSTRALIA": "AU", "AU": "AU",
        "INDIA": "IN", "IN": "IN",
        "GERMANY": "DE", "DE": "DE",
        "FRANCE": "FR", "FR": "FR",
        "NETHERLANDS": "NL", "NL": "NL",
        "SINGAPORE": "SG", "SG": "SG",
        "UAE": "AE", "UNITED ARAB EMIRATES": "AE", "AE": "AE",
    }
    return _MAP.get(_s(country).upper(), "US")


# ── Quality scorer (adaptive to whether SMTP is available) ────────────────────
def _score(r: ContactVerifyResult, email_present: bool) -> float:
    """
    Compute a 0–10 contact-quality score.

    Base weights: email_valid 2 · deliverable 3 · phone 2 · employment 3.

    ADAPTIVE: when SMTP is disabled (deliverability can't be measured), the
    3 "deliverable" points are redistributed so a lead with a valid MX email
    isn't silently capped 3 points lower for a check we never ran. Without this
    fix, EVERY lead on Azure loses 3 points → skews all downstream scoring.
    """
    score = 0.0
    if r.phone_valid:
        score += 2.0
    if r.employment_confirmed:
        score += 3.0

    smtp_measured = _ENABLE_SMTP and _DNS_OK
    if smtp_measured:
        if r.email_valid:
            score += 2.0
        if r.email_deliverable:
            score += 3.0
    else:
        # Deliverability not measurable → weight a valid MX email as worth the
        # full email allotment (2 + 3 = 5) so scoring stays balanced.
        if r.email_valid:
            score += 5.0

    return round(min(score, 10.0), 1)

END FILE: backend/app/validators/contact_verifier.py
==========================================================================================


==========================================================================================
FILE 37/84
PATH: backend/app/validators/icp_validator.py
==========================================================================================

"""
validators/icp_validator.py
───────────────────────────
Stage 1 — ICP Match & Decision-Maker Scoring.  (HARDENED, production-grade)

Single LLM call that:
  1. Semantically matches the lead against the user's ICP prompt
  2. Scores decision-maker authority from job title / seniority
  3. Identifies MAJOR DISQUALIFIERS — mandatory ICP failures that force the
     lead to be a non-match regardless of any positive signals.
  4. Returns structured JSON

Design principle: MUST NOT crash the Celery task, whatever the input or LLM
output. Every field is coerced to a safe type; malformed output degrades to a
safe default instead of raising.

Import:
    from app.validators.icp_validator import validate_icp
    result: ICPResult = validate_icp(lead_data, icp_prompt)
"""

import json
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from app.llm_engine import llm_json, SYSTEM_ICP_VALIDATOR

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

ICP_MATCH_THRESHOLD = 6.0     # icp_match derived from score when LLM is unclear
MAX_REASON_CHARS    = 1000
MAX_LIST_ITEMS      = 30
MAX_ITEM_CHARS      = 200


# ── Seniority / decision-maker classification FRAMEWORK ───────────────────────
# A production system must REASON about ANY job title, not match a fixed list.
# This teaches the model HOW to determine a title's seniority tier and whether
# the person is a decision-maker — so it generalises to messy, compound, and
# previously-unseen titles ("Head of Enterprise Data Management & Analytics -
# Global Digital & Cybersecurity", "Managing Director, AMLCO", etc.).
# _SENIORITY_FRAMEWORK = """
# Classify EVERY job title into ONE seniority tier by REASONING about it — never
# rely on exact string matching. Apply this method to any title, even unseen ones:

# STEP A — Strip noise. Ignore department / function / scope qualifiers that do
# NOT change seniority (e.g. "of Information Security", "EMEA Operations",
# ", Global Digital & Cybersecurity", "AMLCO", "Business Process, Systems and
# Controls", "Advisory Services"). Focus on the SENIORITY KEYWORD.

# STEP B — Map the seniority keyword(s) to a tier:

#   • C-LEVEL  (top decision-maker)
#     - "Chief" + "Officer" in any form → Chief Information Officer, Chief
#       Innovation Officer, Chief Data Officer, Chief AI Officer, etc.
#     - Any C-x-O abbreviation: CEO, CFO, CTO, CIO, CISO, COO, CMO, CRO, CPO,
#       CDO, CHRO — INCLUDING slashed/combined forms (CIO / CISO, CIO/CTO).
#     - President, Founder, Co-Founder, Owner, Proprietor.

#   • SENIOR EXECUTIVE  (top decision-maker; org / business-unit leader)
#     - Managing Director, Partner, Executive Director, General Manager (GM),
#       "Head of <function>" (e.g. Head of IT, Head of IT Procurement, Head of
#       Enterprise Data Management). "Managing Director, <x>" and
#       "Partner, <x>" remain Senior Executive regardless of the trailing text.

#   • VP-LEVEL  (senior decision-maker)
#     - VP, SVP, EVP, AVP, Vice President, "Vice President of <function>".

#   • DIRECTOR  (decision-maker / strong influencer)
#     - Director, Senior Director, "Director of <function>",
#       "Director, <region/scope>".

#   • MANAGER  (mid-level; influencer, sometimes a decision-maker)
#     - Manager, Senior Manager, Group Manager, Team Lead, Supervisor.

#   • INDIVIDUAL CONTRIBUTOR  (usually NOT a decision-maker)
#     - Analyst, Engineer, Developer, Specialist, Coordinator, Associate,
#       Consultant, Administrator, Assistant, Intern.
#     - EXCEPTION: a leadership qualifier ("Lead", "Principal" heading a function,
#       "Staff" leading others) lifts it to Manager-level influence.

# STEP C — Compound / multiple titles. If a title lists several roles or is joined
# by "/", ",", "&", or "and" (e.g. "CIO / CISO", "Managing Director, AMLCO",
# "Partner, Business Process, Systems and Controls"), classify by the HIGHEST tier
# present.

# STEP D — Decision-making authority. C-Level, Senior Executive, VP, and Director
# ARE decision-makers. Managers are partial decision-makers / strong influencers.
# Individual contributors are generally not decision-makers.

# MATCHING RULE — After classifying the tier, compare it to the ICP's target
# seniority. If the classified tier is one the ICP asks for, it IS a seniority
# match. NEVER mark a genuine C-Level / Senior-Executive / VP / Director / Manager
# title as a seniority mismatch when the ICP targets that level. Only flag a
# seniority mismatch when the person is clearly BELOW every level the ICP
# targets (e.g. an individual contributor when the ICP wants Manager and above).
# """

# Decision-maker AUTHORITY SCORE guidance (0–10) — reuses the same tiers.
# _DM_TIER_HINT = """
# Score decision_maker_score from the seniority tier you determined:
#   9–10  C-Level (CEO/CTO/CFO/CIO/CISO/... , President, Founder, Owner)
#   8–9   Senior Executive (Managing Director, Partner, GM, Head of <function>)
#   6–8   VP-Level (VP/SVP/EVP/AVP)
#   5–7   Director (Director / Senior Director)
#   3–4   Manager (Manager / Senior Manager / Team Lead)
#   0–2   Individual Contributor (Analyst, Engineer, Specialist, Associate, Intern)
# Use the HIGHEST tier for compound titles.
# """


# ── Safe coercion helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _s(value, default: str = "") -> str:
    """None-safe string coercion (handles None, numbers, lists, dicts)."""
    if value is None:
        return default
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
        return ", ".join(_s(v) for v in value if v is not None).strip()
    try:
        return str(value).strip()
    except Exception:
        return default


def _as_bool(value) -> bool:
    """Coerce messy LLM truthiness. Fixes bool('false') == True bug."""
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return value
    if value is None:
        return False
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return value != 0
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip().lower() in ("true", "yes", "y", "1", "match", "matched")
    return bool(value)


def _as_float(value, default: float = 0.0) -> float:
    """
    Coerce to float, never raising. Handles None, '8.5', '8.5/10', 'high',
    numeric types, and stray text. Extracts the first number if embedded.
    """
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return 1.0 if value else 0.0
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        try:
            return float(value)
        except Exception:
            return default
    if isinstance(value, str):
        import re
        m = re.search(r"-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?", value)
        if m:
            try:
                return float(m.group())
            except Exception:
                return default
    return default


def _as_list(value) -> list[str]:
    """Coerce to list[str]. A string becomes a single-item list (or split on ,)."""
    if value is None:
        return []
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple, set)):
        out = []
        for item in value:
            t = _s(item)
            if t:
                out.append(t[:MAX_ITEM_CHARS])
            if len(out) >= MAX_LIST_ITEMS:
                break
        return out
    if isinstance(value, str):
        parts = [p.strip() for p in value.replace(";", ",").split(",")]
        return [p[:MAX_ITEM_CHARS] for p in parts if p][:MAX_LIST_ITEMS]
    t = _s(value)
    return [t[:MAX_ITEM_CHARS]] if t else []


def _clamp(value: float, lo: float, hi: float) -> float:
    return max(lo, min(hi, value))


# ── Result dataclass ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@dataclass
class ICPResult:
    icp_match: bool = False
    icp_score: float = 0.0
    matched_criteria: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    mismatches: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    major_disqualifiers: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    decision_maker_score: float = 0.0
    decision_maker_reasoning: str = ""
    raw_output: str = ""

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "icp_match":                self.icp_match,
            "icp_score":                self.icp_score,
            "matched_criteria":         self.matched_criteria,
            "mismatches":               self.mismatches,
            "major_disqualifiers":      self.major_disqualifiers,
            "decision_maker_score":     self.decision_maker_score,
            "decision_maker_reasoning": self.decision_maker_reasoning,
        }


# ── Public API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def validate_icp(lead_data: dict, icp_prompt: str,
                 job_id: str = None, lead_id: str = None) -> ICPResult:
    """
    Run Stage 1 ICP validation. Never raises — safe default on any failure.
    """
    if not isinstance(lead_data, dict):
        logger.warning("validate_icp: lead_data is not a dict — skipping")
        return _safe_default("Invalid lead data")

    icp_prompt = _s(icp_prompt)
    if not icp_prompt:
        logger.warning("validate_icp: empty icp_prompt")
        return _safe_default("No ICP prompt provided")

    prompt = _build_prompt(lead_data, icp_prompt)

    try:
        raw = llm_json(prompt, system=SYSTEM_ICP_VALIDATOR,max_output_tokens=600,
                        stage="icp", module="icp_validator",
                        job_id=job_id, lead_id=lead_id)
        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
            logger.warning(f"validate_icp: LLM returned non-dict ({type(raw)})")
            raw = {}
        result = _parse_response(raw)
        try:
            result.raw_output = json.dumps(raw, default=str)
        except Exception:
            result.raw_output = "{}"
        logger.debug(
            f"ICP [{_s(lead_data.get('Contact Name')) or '?'}] "
            f"match={result.icp_match} icp={result.icp_score} "
            f"dm={result.decision_maker_score} "
            f"disqualifiers={len(result.major_disqualifiers)}"
        )
        return result
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(
            f"ICP validation failed for '{_s(lead_data.get('Contact Name')) or '?'}': {e}"
        )
        return _safe_default(str(e))


# ── Prompt builder ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _build_prompt(lead_data: dict, icp_prompt: str) -> str:
    # Only include non-empty fields — None-safe.
    ICP_RELEVANT = {
        "Contact Name", "Job Title", "Contact Seniority", "Contact Department",
        "Company Name", "Industry", "Sub Industry", "Employee Count",
        "Annual Revenue", "Company Country", "Company State/Region",
        "Email Address", "Phone Number", "LinkedIn Profile URL",
    }
    filled = {
        k: _s(v)[:120]
        for k, v in lead_data.items()
        if _s(v) and k in ICP_RELEVANT
    }
    lead_block = "\n".join(f"  {k}: {v}" for k, v in filled.items()) or "  (no data)"

    return f"""
You are evaluating a B2B sales lead against an Ideal Customer Profile (ICP).

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
ICP DEFINITION (provided by the sales team):
{icp_prompt}

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
LEAD DATA:
{lead_block}

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
EVALUATION SCOPE — EVALUATE ONLY THESE FIVE CRITERIA. NOTHING ELSE.

  1. INDUSTRY        — matches the industry rule in the ICP.
  2. EMPLOYEE COUNT  — within the ICP's employee-count range.
  3. ANNUAL REVENUE  — within the ICP's revenue range.
  4. LOCATION        — within the ICP's target geography.
  5. SENIORITY       — the contact is a DECISION-MAKER (see below).
  6. CONTACT METHOD  — at least ONE valid contact: email OR phone
                       (email preferred; a valid email alone is sufficient).

CRITICAL — DO NOT INVENT CRITERIA. Judge ONLY the six items above. Do NOT
evaluate, reward, or penalise anything else — e.g. non-profit vs for-profit
status, ownership type, technologies used, growth signals, funding, tenure,
department, or any attribute NOT listed above. Such attributes must NEVER
appear in matched_criteria, mismatches, or major_disqualifiers.

SENIORITY RULE (criterion 5):
  - "Decision-maker" = ANY of: C-Level, Senior Executive, VP, Director, or
    Manager (per the CLASSIFICATION FRAMEWORK). Reason about the title.
  - If the Job Title clearly maps to any of these tiers → seniority MATCHES.
  - If the Job Title is MISSING, blank, or genuinely ambiguous → mark it as
    "seniority_unknown" in mismatches, but DO NOT treat it as a disqualifier
    and DO NOT heavily penalise the score for missing data.
  - Only put seniority in major_disqualifiers when the title CLEARLY identifies
    a NON-decision-maker individual contributor (e.g. Analyst, Engineer,
    Coordinator, Associate, Intern) — never for missing/unspecified titles.

CONTACT RULE (criterion 6):
  - A valid email OR a valid phone satisfies this — email is preferred and a
    valid email alone is fully sufficient.
  - Only add "missing_all_valid_contact_methods" to major_disqualifiers when
    BOTH email and phone are missing/invalid. A missing phone with a valid
    email is NOT a disqualifier, and vice-versa.

INSTRUCTIONS:
1. Assess each of the six criteria above using the LEAD DATA. Be semantic —
   handle typos, abbreviations, synonyms, and industry variations.
2. matched_criteria: list ONLY which of the six criteria the lead satisfies,
   using these exact labels where applicable: "industry", "employee_count",
   "annual_revenue", "location", "seniority", "contact_method".
3. mismatches: list ONLY which of the six criteria the lead does NOT satisfy
   or where the data is unknown (use the same labels; use "seniority_unknown"
   for a missing/ambiguous title).
4. major_disqualifiers: only HARD failures among the six — for example
   "non_target_industry", "employee_count_out_of_range", "revenue_out_of_range",
   "outside_target_geography", "non_decision_making_individual_contributor"
   (only for a CLEAR individual contributor), or
   "missing_all_valid_contact_methods" (only when BOTH email AND phone missing).
   Do NOT add disqualifiers for anything outside the six criteria, and do NOT
   disqualify for missing/unspecified seniority.
5. Score decision_maker_score using the AUTHORITY SCORE tiers.
6. If major_disqualifiers is not empty:
   - icp_match MUST be false.
   - icp_score MUST be below 6.0.
7. Otherwise, icp_match = true only if icp_score >= 6.0.

Return this exact JSON structure (no extra keys, no markdown):
{{
  "icp_match":                <true|false>,
  "icp_score":                <float 0.0-10.0>,
  "matched_criteria":         [<string>, ...],
  "mismatches":               [<string>, ...],
  "major_disqualifiers":      [<string>, ...],
  "decision_maker_score":     <float 0.0-10.0>,
  "decision_maker_reasoning": "<one sentence: state the seniority tier you
     classified the title into and why it is/ isn't a decision-maker>"
}}
""".strip()



# ── Response parser (fully type-safe) ─────────────────────────────────────────
def _parse_response(raw: dict) -> ICPResult:
    icp_score = _clamp(_as_float(raw.get("icp_score")), 0.0, 10.0)
    dm_score = _clamp(_as_float(raw.get("decision_maker_score")), 0.0, 10.0)
    major_disqualifiers = _as_list(raw.get("major_disqualifiers"))

    # Mandatory ICP failures override the model's score and match flag.
    if major_disqualifiers:
        icp_score = min(icp_score, ICP_MATCH_THRESHOLD - 0.1)

    icp_match = (
        icp_score >= ICP_MATCH_THRESHOLD
        and not major_disqualifiers
    )

    return ICPResult(
        icp_match=icp_match,
        icp_score=icp_score,
        matched_criteria=_as_list(raw.get("matched_criteria")),
        mismatches=_as_list(raw.get("mismatches")),
        major_disqualifiers=major_disqualifiers,
        decision_maker_score=dm_score,
        decision_maker_reasoning=_s(
            raw.get("decision_maker_reasoning")
        )[:MAX_REASON_CHARS],
    )


def _safe_default(error_msg: str) -> ICPResult:
    r = ICPResult(
        icp_match=False,
        icp_score=0.0,
        matched_criteria=[],
        mismatches=["Validation failed — LLM error"],
        major_disqualifiers=[],
        decision_maker_score=0.0,
        decision_maker_reasoning=f"Error: {_s(error_msg)[:MAX_REASON_CHARS]}",
    )
    try:
        r.raw_output = json.dumps({"error": _s(error_msg)})
    except Exception:
        r.raw_output = "{}"
    return r

END FILE: backend/app/validators/icp_validator.py

PATH: backend/app/validators/web_researcher.py

"""
validators/web_researcher.py
─────────────────────────────
Stage 2 — Web Research & Verification.

Single combined LLM call per lead (replaces 5 separate calls).
Cuts token usage by ~65% vs the original multi-search approach.

Design principle: MUST NOT crash the Celery task. Every field is coerced
to a safe type; the external call is guarded; malformed output degrades
gracefully instead of raising.

Import:
    from app.validators.web_researcher import research_lead
    result: WebResearchResult = research_lead(lead_data)
"""

import json
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from app.llm_engine import llm_json_with_sources, SYSTEM_WEB_RESEARCHER

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── Limits (protect DB columns + token budget) ────────────────────────────────
MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS = 2000
MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS     = 1000
MAX_GROWTH_CHARS      = 2000
MAX_TECHS             = 40
MAX_TECH_LEN          = 80
MAX_SOURCES           = 25


# ── Safe-coercion helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _s(value, default: str = "") -> str:
    if value is None:
        return default
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
        return ", ".join(_s(v) for v in value if v is not None).strip()
    try:
        return str(value).strip()
    except Exception:
        return default


def _as_bool(value) -> bool:
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return value
    if value is None:
        return False
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return value != 0
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip().lower() in ("true", "yes", "y", "1", "confirmed")
    return bool(value)


def _as_tech_list(value) -> list[str]:
    if value is None:
        return []
    if isinstance(value, str):
        parts = []
        for chunk in value.replace(";", ",").replace("\n", ",").split(","):
            t = chunk.strip()
            if t:
                parts.append(t)
        return parts
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple, set)):
        out = []
        for item in value:
            t = _s(item)
            if t:
                out.append(t)
        return out
    t = _s(value)
    return [t] if t else []


def _clip(text: str, limit: int) -> str:
    text = _s(text)
    return text if len(text) <= limit else text[:limit].rstrip() + "…"


# ── Result dataclass ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@dataclass
class WebResearchResult:
    company_verified: bool = False
    person_verified: bool = False
    industry_confirmed: bool = False
    employee_count_confirmed: bool = False
    technologies_found: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    growth_signals: str = ""
    company_summary: str = ""
    company_description: str = ""   # → col 19
    technologies_used: str = ""     # → col 20
    sources: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    raw_output: str = ""

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "company_verified":         self.company_verified,
            "person_verified":          self.person_verified,
            "industry_confirmed":       self.industry_confirmed,
            "employee_count_confirmed": self.employee_count_confirmed,
            "technologies_found":       self.technologies_found,
            "growth_signals":           self.growth_signals,
            "company_summary":          self.company_summary,
            "company_description":      self.company_description,
            "technologies_used":        self.technologies_used,
            "sources":                  self.sources,
        }


# ── Public API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def research_lead(lead_data: dict,
                  job_id: str = None, lead_id: str = None) -> WebResearchResult:
    """
    Run Stage 2 web research using ONE combined LLM+web-search call.
    Never raises — returns safe defaults on any failure.
    """
    if not isinstance(lead_data, dict):
        logger.warning("research_lead: lead_data is not a dict — skipping")
        return _safe_default("Invalid lead data")

    company        = _s(lead_data.get("Company Name"))
    website        = _s(lead_data.get("Company Website"))
    person         = _s(lead_data.get("Contact Name"))
    title          = _s(lead_data.get("Job Title"))
    linkedin       = _s(lead_data.get("LinkedIn Profile URL"))
    industry       = _s(lead_data.get("Industry"))
    employee_count = _s(lead_data.get("Employee Count"))

    if not company:
        logger.warning(f"research_lead: no company name (person='{person}') — skipping")
        return _safe_default("No company name provided")

    website_line  = f"  Website:        {website}\n" if website  else ""
    linkedin_line = f"  LinkedIn:       {linkedin}\n" if linkedin else ""
    website_hint  = (
        "IMPORTANT: Use the Website above to identify the EXACT company and "
        "prioritise information from that domain. Disambiguate it from other "
        "companies with similar names.\n\n"
    ) if website else ""

    prompt = f"""
You are researching a B2B sales lead. Use web search to find ALL the information 
below in ONE SINGLE web search pass. You have a maximum of 1-2 searches total — 
use them wisely by combining all research goals into one query.
DO NOT make separate searches for company, person, tech stack, and growth signals.
Combine everything into one comprehensive search.

LEAD CONTEXT:
  Company:        {company}
{website_line}  Contact:        {person}
{linkedin_line}  Job Title:      {title}
  Industry:       {industry}
  Employee Count: {employee_count}

{website_hint}RESEARCH TASKS (cover all in one search session):
1. COMPANY OVERVIEW — verify employees, revenue range, industry. Confirm or correct the LEAD CONTEXT above.
2. PERSON VERIFY — confirm this person works at this company in this role. If a LinkedIn URL is provided treat it as strong evidence — visit/verify it. Set person_verified=true if LinkedIn OR another credible source confirms employment.
3. TECH STACK — find software, platforms, tools this company uses (job postings, G2/Capterra, BuiltWith, their site).
4. GROWTH SIGNALS — find recent, dated signals: funding, headcount growth, new offices, product launches, acquisitions, or executive hires. Prefer the last 24 months and state dates when available.
5. COMPANY DESCRIPTION — write a 2–3 sentence plain-English description of what this company does, who their customers are, and their value proposition.

Return ONLY this exact JSON (all keys required, use null for unknown fields):
{{
  "company_verified":         <true|false>,
  "person_verified":          <true|false>,
  "industry_confirmed":       <true|false>,
  "employee_count_confirmed": <true|false>,
  "technologies_found":       ["<tech1>", "<tech2>"],
  "technologies_used": "<comma-separated string of tools>",
  "growth_signals":           "<paragraph summary of growth signals, or 'None found'>",
  "company_description":      "<2-3 sentence description>",
  "company_summary":          "<one sentence summary>"
}}
""".strip()

    all_sources: list[str] = []
    partial: dict = {}

    try:
        result = llm_json_with_sources(
            prompt,
            system=SYSTEM_WEB_RESEARCHER,
            max_output_tokens=800,
            stage="web",
            module="web_researcher.combined",
            job_id=job_id,
            lead_id=lead_id,
        )

        if isinstance(result, tuple) and len(result) == 2:
            partial, urls = result
        else:
            partial, urls = (result if isinstance(result, dict) else {}), []

        if not isinstance(partial, dict):
            partial = {}
        if not isinstance(urls, (list, tuple)):
            urls = []

        all_sources = list(urls)

    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning(f"Web research combined call failed for '{company}': {e}")

    if not partial:
        d = _safe_default("Combined web search failed")
        d.sources = all_sources[:MAX_SOURCES]
        return d

    merged = _merge([partial], all_sources)
    try:
        merged.raw_output = json.dumps(merged.to_dict(), default=str)
    except Exception:
        merged.raw_output = "{}"

    logger.debug(
        f"Web research [{company}] verified={merged.company_verified} "
        f"person={merged.person_verified} techs={len(merged.technologies_found)} "
        f"sources={len(merged.sources)}"
    )
    return merged


# ── Merger (fully type-safe) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _merge(partials: list[dict], sources: list[str]) -> WebResearchResult:
    result = WebResearchResult()
    seen: set[str] = set()
    clean_sources: list[str] = []
    for s in (sources or []):
        s = _s(s)
        if s and s not in seen:
            seen.add(s)
            clean_sources.append(s)
    result.sources = clean_sources[:MAX_SOURCES]

    for p in partials:
        if not isinstance(p, dict):
            continue

        result.company_verified         |= _as_bool(p.get("company_verified"))
        result.person_verified          |= _as_bool(p.get("person_verified"))
        result.industry_confirmed       |= _as_bool(p.get("industry_confirmed"))
        result.employee_count_confirmed |= _as_bool(p.get("employee_count_confirmed"))

        for tech in _as_tech_list(p.get("technologies_found")):
            t = _clip(tech, MAX_TECH_LEN)
            if t and t.lower() not in {x.lower() for x in result.technologies_found}:
                if len(result.technologies_found) < MAX_TECHS:
                    result.technologies_found.append(t)

        if not result.growth_signals:
            g = _s(p.get("growth_signals"))
            if g and g.lower() not in ("none", "none found", "n/a", "null"):
                result.growth_signals = _clip(g, MAX_GROWTH_CHARS)

        if not result.company_summary:
            s = _s(p.get("company_summary"))
            if s:
                result.company_summary = _clip(s, MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS)

        if not result.company_description:
            d = _s(p.get("company_description"))
            if d:
                result.company_description = _clip(d, MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS)

    result.technologies_used = ", ".join(result.technologies_found)

    if not result.company_description and result.company_summary:
        result.company_description = result.company_summary

    return result


# ── Safe helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def _safe_default(reason: str) -> WebResearchResult:
    r = WebResearchResult()
    r.growth_signals = f"Web research skipped: {reason}"
    try:
        r.raw_output = json.dumps({"error": reason})
    except Exception:
        r.raw_output = "{}"
    return r

END FILE: backend/app/validators/web_researcher.py

"""
vectorstore/embedder.py
───────────────────────
Chunking + embedding helpers for the RAG ingestion pipeline.

  chunk_text(text)  → list[str]      word-based chunks with overlap
  embed(texts)      → list[vector]   batched Azure/OpenAI embeddings

Sizing: ~600 tokens/chunk with ~100 overlap is the RAG sweet spot for
website copy. We approximate tokens with words (~0.75 token/word) to avoid a
tokenizer dependency; tune CHUNK_WORDS if you prefer tiktoken.
"""

import logging

from openai import AzureOpenAI

from app.config import settings

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

CHUNK_WORDS   = 450      # ≈ 600 tokens
OVERLAP_WORDS = 75       # ≈ 100 tokens
EMBED_BATCH   = 96       # inputs per embeddings API call


# ── Embedding client (reuse your existing Azure OpenAI setup) ─────────────────
_embed_client: AzureOpenAI | None = None


def _client() -> AzureOpenAI:
    global _embed_client
    if _embed_client is None:
        _embed_client = AzureOpenAI(
            api_key=settings.AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY,
            api_version=settings.AZURE_OPENAI_API_VERSION,
            azure_endpoint=settings.AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT,
        )
    return _embed_client


# ── Chunking ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def chunk_text(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Split cleaned text into overlapping word windows. Empty → []."""
    if not text or not text.strip():
        return []
    words = text.split()
    if len(words) <= CHUNK_WORDS:
        return [" ".join(words)]

    chunks: list[str] = []
    step = CHUNK_WORDS - OVERLAP_WORDS
    for start in range(0, len(words), step):
        window = words[start:start + CHUNK_WORDS]
        if window:
            chunks.append(" ".join(window))
        if start + CHUNK_WORDS >= len(words):
            break
    return chunks


# ── Embedding ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def embed(texts: list[str]) -> list[list[float]]:
    """Batch-embed a list of strings → list of vectors (same order)."""
    if not texts:
        return []
    client = _client()
    logger.warning(f"[embed] endpoint={settings.AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT!r}")
    logger.warning(f"[embed] api_version={settings.AZURE_OPENAI_API_VERSION!r}")
    logger.warning(f"[embed] deployment={settings.EMBED_DEPLOYMENT!r}")
    out: list[list[float]] = []
    for i in range(0, len(texts), EMBED_BATCH):
        batch = texts[i:i + EMBED_BATCH]
        resp = client.embeddings.create(
            model=settings.EMBED_DEPLOYMENT,  
            input=batch,
        )
        out.extend(d.embedding for d in resp.data)
    return out


def embed_one(text: str) -> list[float]:
    """Embed a single query string."""
    return embed([text])[0] if text and text.strip() else []

END FILE: backend/app/vectorstore/embedder.py
==========================================================================================

FILE 41/84
PATH: backend/app/vectorstore/qdrant_store.py
==========================================================================================

"""
vectorstore/qdrant_store.py
───────────────────────────
Qdrant Cloud wrapper for the chatbot RAG feature.

Design decisions (per Qdrant multitenancy best practice):
  • ONE collection for everything: "company_chunks".
  • Each point is partitioned by a `job_id` payload field (the "session").
  • `job_id` is a TENANT payload index (is_tenant=True) → Qdrant co-locates a
    job's vectors on disk for fast sequential reads.
  • HNSW is built PER TENANT (payload_m=16, m=0) instead of one global graph,
    which massively speeds up ingestion when many jobs share the collection.
  • `company_name` is also indexed so "…for this company" queries pre-filter.

Every read/write is HARD-SCOPED to a job_id, so one session can never see
another session's data.

Env / settings required (add to app/config.py):
    QDRANT_URL            = "https://xxxx.cloud.qdrant.io:6333"
    QDRANT_API_KEY        = "..."
    QDRANT_COLLECTION     = "company_chunks"
    EMBED_DIM             = 1536          # text-embedding-3-small
"""

import logging
import uuid
from typing import Optional

from qdrant_client import QdrantClient
from qdrant_client.http import models as qm

from app.config import settings

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

COLLECTION = settings.QDRANT_COLLECTION


# ── Singleton client ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_client: Optional[QdrantClient] = None


def get_client() -> QdrantClient:
    """Lazy singleton Qdrant Cloud client (thread-safe enough for our worker)."""
    global _client
    if _client is None:
        _client = QdrantClient(
            url=settings.QDRANT_URL,
            api_key=settings.QDRANT_API_KEY,
            timeout=30,
            prefer_grpc=False,
        )
    return _client


# ── One-time collection bootstrap ─────────────────────────────────────────────
def ensure_collection() -> None:
    """
    Create the shared collection + payload indexes if they don't exist.
    Call this ONCE at app/worker startup (idempotent).
    """
    client = get_client()

    exists = client.collection_exists(COLLECTION)
    if not exists:
        client.create_collection(
            collection_name=COLLECTION,
            vectors_config=qm.VectorParams(
                size=settings.EMBED_DIM,
                distance=qm.Distance.COSINE,
            ),
            # Per-tenant HNSW: disable the global graph (m=0), build per group.
            hnsw_config=qm.HnswConfigDiff(payload_m=16, m=0),
        )
        logger.info(f"Created Qdrant collection '{COLLECTION}'")

    # job_id → tenant index (co-locates a session's vectors on disk).
    client.create_payload_index(
        collection_name=COLLECTION,
        field_name="job_id",
        field_schema=qm.KeywordIndexParams(
            type=qm.KeywordIndexType.KEYWORD,
            is_tenant=True,
        ),
    )
    # company_name → keyword index for "…for this company" filtering.
    client.create_payload_index(
        collection_name=COLLECTION,
        field_name="company_name",
        field_schema=qm.PayloadSchemaType.KEYWORD,
    )
    logger.info("Qdrant payload indexes ensured (job_id tenant, company_name)")


# ── Ingestion ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def upsert_chunks(
    job_id: str,
    lead_id: str,
    company_name: str,
    chunks: list[str],
    vectors: list[list[float]],
    website_url: str = "",
    batch_size: int = 128,
) -> int:
    """
    Insert a company's chunk embeddings, tagged with the session job_id.
    Batches at 128 points (Qdrant sweet spot 64–256).
    Returns number of points written.
    """
    if not chunks:
        return 0

    client = get_client()
    points: list[qm.PointStruct] = []
    for text, vec in zip(chunks, vectors):
        points.append(
            qm.PointStruct(
                id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
                vector=vec,
                payload={
                    "job_id":       job_id,        # ← session scope
                    "lead_id":      lead_id,
                    "company_name": company_name,  # ← company scope
                    "website_url":  website_url,
                    "chunk_text":   text,
                },
            )
        )

    written = 0
    for i in range(0, len(points), batch_size):
        client.upsert(collection_name=COLLECTION, points=points[i:i + batch_size])
        written += len(points[i:i + batch_size])

    logger.info(f"Upserted {written} chunks for '{company_name}' (job={job_id})")
    return written


# ── Retrieval (always scoped to a job_id) ─────────────────────────────────────
def search(
    job_id: str,
    query_vector: list[float],
    company_name: Optional[str] = None,
    limit: int = 6,
) -> list[dict]:
    """
    Semantic search WITHIN a single session (job_id). Optionally narrow to one
    company. Returns [{score, chunk_text, company_name, website_url}, ...].
    """
    client = get_client()

    must = [qm.FieldCondition(key="job_id", match=qm.MatchValue(value=job_id))]
    if company_name:
        must.append(
            qm.FieldCondition(key="company_name",
                              match=qm.MatchValue(value=company_name))
        )

    hits = client.query_points(
        collection_name=COLLECTION,
        query=query_vector,
        query_filter=qm.Filter(must=must),
        limit=limit,
        with_payload=True,
    ).points

    return [
        {
            "score":        h.score,
            "chunk_text":   h.payload.get("chunk_text", ""),
            "company_name": h.payload.get("company_name", ""),
            "website_url":  h.payload.get("website_url", ""),
        }
        for h in hits
    ]


def delete_job(job_id: str) -> None:
    """Remove all vectors for a session (call from DELETE /jobs/{job_id})."""
    client = get_client()
    client.delete(
        collection_name=COLLECTION,
        points_selector=qm.FilterSelector(
            filter=qm.Filter(
                must=[qm.FieldCondition(key="job_id",
                                        match=qm.MatchValue(value=job_id))]
            )
        ),
    )
    logger.info(f"Deleted all Qdrant vectors for job {job_id}")

END FILE: backend/app/vectorstore/qdrant_store.py

-- Run once against the existing Azure SQL database before deploying this version.
IF COL_LENGTH('dbo.final_scores', 'outreach_intelligence') IS NULL
    ALTER TABLE dbo.final_scores ADD outreach_intelligence NVARCHAR(MAX) NULL;

IF COL_LENGTH('dbo.jobs', 'failed_rows') IS NULL
    ALTER TABLE dbo.jobs ADD failed_rows INT NOT NULL CONSTRAINT DF_jobs_failed_rows DEFAULT 0;

IF COL_LENGTH('dbo.leads', 'processing_status') IS NULL
    ALTER TABLE dbo.leads ADD processing_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL CONSTRAINT DF_leads_processing_status DEFAULT 'pending';

IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.indexes WHERE name = 'IX_jobs_created_at' AND object_id = OBJECT_ID('dbo.jobs'))
    CREATE INDEX IX_jobs_created_at ON dbo.jobs(created_at DESC);

IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.indexes WHERE name = 'IX_leads_job_row' AND object_id = OBJECT_ID('dbo.leads'))
    CREATE INDEX IX_leads_job_row ON dbo.leads(job_id, row_index) INCLUDE (company_name, contact_name, email_address);

IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.indexes WHERE name = 'UX_final_scores_lead' AND object_id = OBJECT_ID('dbo.final_scores'))
BEGIN
    ;WITH duplicates AS (
        SELECT id, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY lead_id ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC) AS rn
        FROM dbo.final_scores
    )
    DELETE FROM duplicates WHERE rn > 1;
    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX UX_final_scores_lead ON dbo.final_scores(lead_id);
END;

IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.indexes WHERE name = 'IX_final_scores_priority_score' AND object_id = OBJECT_ID('dbo.final_scores'))
    CREATE INDEX IX_final_scores_priority_score ON dbo.final_scores(priority, total_score DESC) INCLUDE (lead_id);

IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sys.indexes WHERE name = 'IX_validation_lead_stage' AND object_id = OBJECT_ID('dbo.validation_results'))
BEGIN
    ;WITH duplicates AS (
        SELECT id, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY lead_id, stage ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC) AS rn
        FROM dbo.validation_results
    )
    DELETE FROM duplicates WHERE rn > 1;
    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IX_validation_lead_stage ON dbo.validation_results(lead_id, stage);
END;

# Quality Lead API and workers

FastAPI accepts uploads and serves job/results APIs. Celery performs imports, lead qualification, web research, scoring, outreach generation, and embeddings asynchronously.

Start locally after configuring `.env`:

```powershell
uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000
celery -A app.celery_app.celery worker --loglevel=info -Q lead_processing,lead_imports,lead_embeddings --pool=solo
```

See `../dev.md` for local setup, `../read.md` for the end-to-end design, and `../AZURE_DEPLOYMENT.md` for production deployment.

"""Create or rotate an admin password using bcrypt."""
import getpass

import bcrypt

from app.database import db_session
from app.models import AdminUser


def main() -> None:
    username = input("Admin username: ").strip()
    password = getpass.getpass("Admin password: ")
    if not username or len(password) < 12 or len(password.encode("utf-8")) > 72:
        raise SystemExit("Username is required and password must be 12–72 UTF-8 bytes.")
    password_hash = bcrypt.hashpw(password.encode(), bcrypt.gensalt(rounds=12)).decode()
    with db_session() as db:
        user = db.query(AdminUser).filter(AdminUser.username == username).first()
        if user:
            user.password = password_hash
        else:
            db.add(AdminUser(username=username, password=password_hash))
    print(f"Admin '{username}' is ready.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

END FILE: backend/scripts/create_admin.py

import pandas as pd
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from app.auth import create_access_token, verify_access_token
from app.main import app
from app.scoring.scoring_engine import _parse_response
from app.utils.deduplicator import deduplicate


def test_signed_token_round_trip():
    assert verify_access_token(create_access_token("admin"))["sub"] == "admin"


def test_public_health_and_protected_jobs():
    client = TestClient(app, base_url="http://localhost")
    assert client.get("/health/live").status_code == 200
    assert client.get("/jobs").status_code == 401


def test_openapi_exposes_outreach_intelligence():
    client = TestClient(app, base_url="http://localhost")
    schema = client.get("/openapi.json").json()
    properties = schema["components"]["schemas"]["LeadResultResponse"]["properties"]
    assert "outreach_intelligence" in properties


def test_dedup_is_lossless_for_similar_distinct_names():
    frame = pd.DataFrame({
        "CRM Contact ID": ["", "", ""],
        "Email Address": ["", "", "same@example.com"],
        "Contact Name": ["Person 1", "Person 10", "Person 1"],
        "Company Name": ["Acme", "Acme", "Acme"],
    })
    result, stats = deduplicate(frame)
    assert list(result["Contact Name"]) == ["Person 1", "Person 10"]
    assert stats["duplicates_removed"] == 1


def test_backend_computes_weighted_total_and_serializes_outreach():
    result = _parse_response({
        "icp_score": 80,
        "authority_score": 70,
        "data_score": 60,
        "growth_score": 50,
        "contact_score": 40,
        "reasoning": "Evidence-backed summary",
        "growth_signals": "Recent hiring",
        "outreach_intelligence": {"recommended_channel": "email"},
    })
    assert result.total_score == 66.0
    assert '"recommended_channel": "email"' in result.outreach_intelligence

FILE 49/84
PATH: extract.py

from __future__ import annotations
from datetime import datetime
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path


# ============================================================
# CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================

# Files that are useful for an LLM to understand the project.
ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS = {
    # Python
    ".py",

    # JavaScript / TypeScript
    ".js",
    ".jsx",
    ".ts",
    ".tsx",
    ".mjs",
    ".cjs",

    # Web
    ".html",
    ".htm",
    ".css",
    ".scss",
    ".sass",

    # Config / data
    ".json",
    ".yaml",
    ".yml",
    ".toml",
    ".ini",

    # SQL / migrations
    ".sql",

    # Shell
    ".sh",
    ".ps1",
    ".bat",
    ".cmd",

    # Documentation
    ".md",
    ".txt",
}


# Directories that should never be extracted.
EXCLUDED_DIRS = {
    "__pycache__",
    ".git",
    ".github",
    ".idea",
    ".vscode",

    "venv",
    ".venv",
    "env",
    ".env",

    "node_modules",

    ".next",
    "dist",
    "build",
    "out",

    ".pytest_cache",
    ".mypy_cache",
    ".ruff_cache",

    "coverage",
    ".coverage",

    "logs",
    "tmp",
    "temp",

    "site-packages",

    "htmlcov",
    "extracted_Codes",
}


# Specific files that should never be included.
EXCLUDED_FILES = {
    ".env",
    ".env.local",
    ".env.development",
    ".env.production",
    ".env.staging",

    ".gitignore",

    "package-lock.json",
    "pnpm-lock.yaml",
    "yarn.lock",

    "poetry.lock",

    # Don't recursively include previously generated extraction files.
    "codes.txt",
}


# Patterns for sensitive files.
SENSITIVE_FILE_PREFIXES = {
    ".env.",
}


# Maximum individual source file size.
# Prevents accidentally dumping giant generated files.
MAX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024  # 2 MB


# ============================================================
# HELPERS
# ============================================================

def should_exclude_directory(path: Path) -> bool:
    """Return True if directory should not be scanned."""

    return path.name.lower() in {
        name.lower() for name in EXCLUDED_DIRS
    }


def should_exclude_file(path: Path) -> bool:
    """Return True if a file should not be included."""

    name = path.name
    lower_name = name.lower()

    # Exact excluded files
    if lower_name in {
        item.lower() for item in EXCLUDED_FILES
    }:
        return True

    # Generated output files
    if lower_name.endswith("_codes.txt"):
        return True

    # Environment files
    if lower_name == ".env":
        return True

    if lower_name.startswith(".env."):
        return True

    # Python bytecode
    if path.suffix.lower() in {
        ".pyc",
        ".pyo",
    }:
        return True

    return False


def is_allowed_source_file(path: Path) -> bool:
    """Check if this file should be included."""

    if not path.is_file():
        return False

    if should_exclude_file(path):
        return False

    if path.suffix.lower() not in ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS:
        return False

    try:
        if path.stat().st_size > MAX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES:
            print(
                f"[SKIP] File too large: {path}"
            )
            return False
    except OSError:
        return False

    return True


def normalise_cli_target(raw_target: str) -> str:
    """
    Supports both:

        python extract.py app/api/auth

    and:

        python extract.py --app/api/auth

        python extract.py --app
    """

    raw_target = raw_target.strip()

    if raw_target.startswith("--"):
        raw_target = raw_target[2:]

    # Allow forward slash syntax on Windows.
    raw_target = raw_target.replace("/", os.sep)

    return raw_target


def resolve_target(raw_target: str) -> Path:
    """Resolve requested path intelligently."""

    cleaned = normalise_cli_target(raw_target)

    if cleaned in {
        "",
        ".",
    }:
        return Path.cwd().resolve()

    candidate = Path(cleaned)

    # First try relative to current directory.
    if candidate.exists():
        return candidate.resolve()

    # Check whether user typed current root folder name.
    cwd = Path.cwd()

    if cleaned.lower() == cwd.name.lower():
        return cwd.resolve()

    # Check parent directory.
    parent_candidate = cwd.parent / cleaned

    if parent_candidate.exists():
        return parent_candidate.resolve()

    raise FileNotFoundError(
        f"Target not found: {raw_target}"
    )


def collect_source_files(target: Path) -> list[Path]:
    """Recursively collect source files."""

    if target.is_file():
        if is_allowed_source_file(target):
            return [target]

        raise ValueError(
            f"Unsupported or excluded file: {target}"
        )

    collected: list[Path] = []

    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(target):
        root_path = Path(root)

        # Prevent traversal into excluded directories.
        dirs[:] = [
            directory
            for directory in dirs
            if not should_exclude_directory(
                root_path / directory
            )
        ]

        for file_name in files:
            file_path = root_path / file_name

            if is_allowed_source_file(file_path):
                collected.append(file_path)

    # Stable output ordering makes diffs easier.
    return sorted(
        collected,
        key=lambda p: str(p).lower(),
    )


def read_text_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    """
    Read text safely.

    UTF-8 is attempted first and common Windows fallback second.
    """

    encodings = [
        "utf-8",
        "utf-8-sig",
        "cp1252",
    ]

    for encoding in encodings:
        try:
            return path.read_text(
                encoding=encoding
            )
        except UnicodeDecodeError:
            continue
        except OSError as exc:
            print(
                f"[ERROR] Could not read {path}: {exc}"
            )
            return None

    print(
        f"[SKIP] Unable to decode: {path}"
    )

    return None


def get_display_path(
    file_path: Path,
    target: Path,
) -> str:
    """Create an LLM-friendly relative path."""

    if target.is_file():
        return file_path.name

    try:
        relative = file_path.relative_to(target)
    except ValueError:
        relative = file_path

    return relative.as_posix()

def generate_tree(target: Path) -> str:
    """
    Generate a tree structure for the target directory.
    Excluded directories/files are ignored.
    """

    lines: list[str] = []

    root_name = target.name if target.name else str(target)
    lines.append(f"{root_name}/")

    def walk(directory: Path, prefix: str = "") -> None:
        entries = []

        try:
            for entry in directory.iterdir():
                if entry.is_dir():
                    if should_exclude_directory(entry):
                        continue
                elif entry.is_file():
                    if should_exclude_file(entry):
                        continue

                entries.append(entry)

        except PermissionError:
            return

        entries.sort(
            key=lambda p: (
                not p.is_dir(),
                p.name.lower(),
            )
        )

        for index, entry in enumerate(entries):
            is_last = index == len(entries) - 1

            connector = "└── " if is_last else "├── "

            if entry.is_dir():
                lines.append(
                    f"{prefix}{connector}{entry.name}/"
                )
            else:
                lines.append(
                    f"{prefix}{connector}{entry.name}"
                )

            if entry.is_dir():
                extension = "    " if is_last else "│   "

                walk(
                    entry,
                    prefix + extension,
                )

    if target.is_dir():
        walk(target)

    return "\n".join(lines)

def create_tree_output_filename(target: Path) -> Path:
    if target.is_file():
        base_name = target.stem
    else:
        base_name = target.name

    if not base_name:
        base_name = "project"

    safe_name = "".join(
        char if char.isalnum() or char in "-_"
        else "_"
        for char in base_name
    )

    output_dir = Path.cwd() / "extracted_Codes"
    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    ist_now = datetime.now(
        ZoneInfo("Asia/Kolkata")
    )

    timestamp = ist_now.strftime(
        "%d-%m-%Y_%I-%M_%p"
    )

    filename = (
        f"{safe_name}_tree_{timestamp}.txt"
    )

    return output_dir / filename

def export_tree(target: Path) -> None:
    if not target.is_dir():
        raise ValueError(
            "--tree requires a directory."
        )

    tree_text = generate_tree(target)

    output_file = create_tree_output_filename(
        target
    )

    output_file.write_text(
        tree_text,
        encoding="utf-8",
    )

    print()
    print("=" * 60)
    print("TREE EXTRACTION COMPLETE")
    print("=" * 60)
    print(f"Source : {target}")
    print(f"Output : {output_file}")
    print("=" * 60)


def create_output_filename(target: Path) -> Path:
    """
    Creates output inside extracted_Codes/ with IST timestamp.

    Example:
        auth_codes_22-08-2026_11-15_AM.txt
    """

    if target.is_file():
        base_name = target.stem
    else:
        base_name = target.name

    if not base_name:
        base_name = "project"

    safe_name = "".join(
        char if char.isalnum() or char in "-_"
        else "_"
        for char in base_name
    )

    # Create output directory
    output_dir = Path.cwd() / "extracted_Codes"
    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # Current IST date/time
    ist_now = datetime.now(ZoneInfo("Asia/Kolkata"))

    timestamp = ist_now.strftime("%d-%m-%Y_%I-%M_%p")

    filename = f"{safe_name}_codes_{timestamp}.txt"

    return output_dir / filename

# ============================================================
# EXTRACTION
# ============================================================

def extract_project(
    target: Path,
    output_file: Path,
) -> None:

    source_files = collect_source_files(target)

    if not source_files:
        print(
            f"No supported source files found in: {target}"
        )
        return

    output_parts: list[str] = []

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Header
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    output_parts.append(
        "=" * 90
    )
    output_parts.append(
        "PROJECT SOURCE CODE EXPORT"
    )
    output_parts.append(
        "=" * 90
    )

    output_parts.append(
        f"Source: {target}"
    )

    output_parts.append(
        f"Files: {len(source_files)}"
    )

    output_parts.append("")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Project file index
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    output_parts.append(
        "=" * 90
    )
    output_parts.append(
        "FILE INDEX"
    )
    output_parts.append(
        "=" * 90
    )
    output_parts.append("")

    for file_path in source_files:
        display_path = get_display_path(
            file_path,
            target,
        )

        output_parts.append(
            display_path
        )

    output_parts.append("")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # File contents
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    for index, file_path in enumerate(
        source_files,
        start=1,
    ):
        content = read_text_file(file_path)

        if content is None:
            continue

        display_path = get_display_path(
            file_path,
            target,
        )

        output_parts.extend(
            [
                "",
                "=" * 90,
                f"FILE {index}/{len(source_files)}",
                f"PATH: {display_path}",
                "=" * 90,
                "",
                content.rstrip(),
                "",
                f"END FILE: {display_path}",
                "=" * 90,
                "",
            ]
        )

    final_output = "\n".join(
        output_parts
    )

    output_file.write_text(
        final_output,
        encoding="utf-8",
    )

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Result
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    total_size = output_file.stat().st_size

    print()
    print("=" * 60)
    print("SOURCE EXTRACTION COMPLETE")
    print("=" * 60)

    print(
        f"Source       : {target}"
    )

    print(
        f"Files        : {len(source_files)}"
    )

    print(
        f"Output       : {output_file}"
    )

    print(
        f"Output size  : {total_size / 1024:.2f} KB"
    )

    print("=" * 60)


# ============================================================
# CLI
# ============================================================

def print_help() -> None:

    script_name = Path(
        sys.argv[0]
    ).name

    print(
        f"""
Project Source Code Extractor

Usage:

    python {script_name} <folder>

Examples:

    python {script_name} app/api/auth
    python {script_name} app/api/v1
    python {script_name} app
    python {script_name} .
    python {script_name} backend_freight

Alternative syntax:

    python {script_name} --app/api/auth
    python {script_name} --app/api/v1
    python {script_name} --app
    python {script_name} --backend_freight

Output examples:

    app/api/auth
        -> auth_codes.txt

    app
        -> app_codes.txt

    backend_freight
        -> backend_freight_codes.txt
"""
    )


def main() -> None:

    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print_help()
        sys.exit(1)

    argument = sys.argv[1]
    tree_mode = "--tree" in sys.argv[2:]

    if argument in {
        "-h",
        "--help",
        "help",
    }:
        print_help()
        return

    try:
        target = resolve_target(argument)

        if tree_mode:
            export_tree(target)
            return

        output_file = create_output_filename(
            target
        )

        # Prevent output being included in itself.
        if (
            target.is_file()
            and target.resolve()
            == output_file.resolve()
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Output file cannot be the source file."
            )

        extract_project(
            target,
            output_file,
        )

    except FileNotFoundError as exc:
        print(
            f"[ERROR] {exc}"
        )
        sys.exit(1)

    except ValueError as exc:
        print(
            f"[ERROR] {exc}"
        )
        sys.exit(1)

    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print(
            "\nExtraction cancelled."
        )
        sys.exit(130)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

END FILE: extract.py

# LeadQual AI — Frontend

A production-grade React frontend for an AI Lead Qualification Platform, wired to a FastAPI backend.

## Features

- **Login** — expiring signed-token authentication
- **Dashboard** — server-aggregated KPI cards and live asynchronous-job lobby
- **Upload Flow** — drag-and-drop import, ICP prompt, live progress, and leave-to-dashboard action
- **Job Detail** — globally searchable/sortable/filterable paginated results, retry/resume ICP, lead radar, and Outreach Intelligence
- **Chatbot** — floating session-scoped assistant with source links and tool tags
- **Real-time polling** — React Query with `placeholderData` for flicker-free refreshes

## Getting Started

```bash
# 1. Configure the backend URL
cp .env.example .env
# edit .env → VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000

# 2. Install dependencies
npm ci

# 3. Start the dev server
npm run dev
```

The app runs at `http://localhost:5173` and calls the configured FastAPI URL directly.

## Environment Variables

| Variable             | Default                | Description                |
|----------------------|------------------------|----------------------------|
| `VITE_API_BASE_URL`  | `http://127.0.0.1:8000`| FastAPI backend base URL   |

## Tech Stack

- React + Vite + TypeScript
- Tailwind CSS (custom theme)
- React Router (navigation)
- TanStack React Query (data fetching & polling)
- Axios (HTTP client with auth interceptors)
- Recharts (charts)
- Framer Motion (animations)
- lucide-react (icons)

## API Endpoints Used

| Method | Endpoint                        | Purpose                     |
|--------|---------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| POST   | `/auth/login`                   | Authenticate admin          |
| GET    | `/health`                       | Backend health check        |
| POST   | `/upload`                       | Upload lead file            |
| POST   | `/jobs/{id}/icp`                | Start ICP processing        |
| GET    | `/jobs`                         | List all jobs               |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/status`             | Job progress status         |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/results`            | Job lead results            |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/results/{lead_id}`  | Single lead detail          |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/export/excel`       | Export to Excel             |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/export/csv`         | Export to CSV               |
| DELETE | `/jobs/{id}`                    | Delete a job                |
| POST   | `/jobs/{id}/chat`               | Send chat message           |
| GET    | `/jobs/{id}/chat/history`       | Chat history                |

END FILE: frontend/README.md

import { apiClient } from '@/lib/apiClient';
import type {
  LoginResponse,
  HealthResponse,
  UploadResponse,
  IcpResponse,
  JobStatusResponse,
  JobSummary,
  LeadResult,
  ChatResponse,
  ChatMessage,
  DashboardStatsResponse,
} from '@/types';

// ── Auth ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const login = async (username: string, password: string): Promise<LoginResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.post<LoginResponse>('/auth/login', { username, password });
  return data;
};

// ── Health ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const getHealth = async (): Promise<HealthResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<HealthResponse>('/health');
  return data;
};

// ── Jobs / Upload pipeline ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const uploadFile = async (file: File): Promise<UploadResponse> => {
  const form = new FormData();
  form.append('file', file);
  const { data } = await apiClient.post<UploadResponse>('/upload', form, {
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'multipart/form-data' },
    timeout: 120000,   // headroom for the initial file transfer of large files
  });
  return data;         // { job_id, status: "importing", ... }
};


export const startIcp = async (jobId: string, icpPrompt: string): Promise<IcpResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.post<IcpResponse>(`/jobs/${jobId}/icp`, { icp_prompt: icpPrompt });
  return data;
};

export const getJobs = async (): Promise<JobSummary[]> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<JobSummary[]>('/jobs');
  return data ?? [];
};

export const getDashboardStats = async (): Promise<DashboardStatsResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<DashboardStatsResponse>('/dashboard/stats');
  return data;
};

export const getJobStatus = async (jobId: string): Promise<JobStatusResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<JobStatusResponse>(`/jobs/${jobId}/status`);
  return data;
};

// ── Results (paginated) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Backend returns: { items, total, limit, offset, returned, has_more }
export interface PaginatedLeads {
  items:    LeadResult[];
  total:    number;   // total leads for the whole job
  limit:    number;   // page size used
  offset:   number;   // starting index of this page
  returned: number;   // rows in this page
  has_more: boolean;  // whether more pages exist after this one
}

/**
 * Fetch ONE page of results. Used by the results table for windowed,
 * server-side pagination (only loads what the user is viewing).
 */
export const getResultsPage = async (
  jobId: string,
  offset = 0,
  limit = 50,
  filters?: { search?: string; priority?: string; sortBy?: string; sortDir?: string },
): Promise<PaginatedLeads> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get(`/jobs/${jobId}/results`, {
    params: {
      limit, offset,
      search: filters?.search || undefined,
      priority: filters?.priority === 'all' ? undefined : filters?.priority,
      sort_by: filters?.sortBy,
      sort_dir: filters?.sortDir,
    },
  });
  return {
    items:    data?.items ?? [],
    total:    data?.total ?? 0,
    limit:    data?.limit ?? limit,
    offset:   data?.offset ?? offset,
    returned: data?.returned ?? (data?.items?.length ?? 0),
    has_more: data?.has_more ?? false,
  };
};

/**
 * Fetch ALL results by looping through every page.
 * Use ONLY where the full set is genuinely needed (e.g. dashboard KPIs) —
 * NOT for the table, which should use getResultsPage for windowed loading.
 */
export const getJobResults = async (jobId: string): Promise<LeadResult[]> => {
  const all: LeadResult[] = [];
  let offset = 0;
  const limit = 200;
  // Safety cap so a bad has_more can never infinite-loop.
  for (let guard = 0; guard < 1000; guard++) {
    const page = await getResultsPage(jobId, offset, limit);
    all.push(...page.items);
    if (!page.has_more || page.items.length === 0) break;
    offset += limit;
  }
  return all;
};

export const getLeadDetail = async (jobId: string, leadId: string): Promise<LeadResult> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<LeadResult>(`/jobs/${jobId}/results/${leadId}`);
  return data;
};

export const deleteJob = async (jobId: string): Promise<void> => {
  await apiClient.delete(`/jobs/${jobId}`);
};

export const exportExcel = (jobId: string): string =>
  `${apiClient.defaults.baseURL}/jobs/${jobId}/export/excel`;

export const exportCsv = (jobId: string): string =>
  `${apiClient.defaults.baseURL}/jobs/${jobId}/export/csv`;

// Download export with auth header
export const downloadExport = async (jobId: string, format: 'excel' | 'csv'): Promise<void> => {
  const ext = format === 'excel' ? 'excel' : 'csv';
  const resp = await apiClient.get(`/jobs/${jobId}/export/${ext}`, {
    responseType: 'blob',
  });
  const disposition = resp.headers['content-disposition'] ?? '';
  const match = disposition.match(/filename="?([^";\n]+)"?/);
  const filename = match?.[1] ?? `job_${jobId}.${format === 'excel' ? 'xlsx' : 'csv'}`;
  const url = window.URL.createObjectURL(resp.data);
  const a = document.createElement('a');
  a.href = url;
  a.download = filename;
  document.body.appendChild(a);
  a.click();
  document.body.removeChild(a);
  window.URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
};

// ── Chat ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const sendChat = async (jobId: string, message: string): Promise<ChatResponse> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.post<ChatResponse>(`/jobs/${jobId}/chat`, { message });
  return data;
};

export const getChatHistory = async (jobId: string): Promise<ChatMessage[]> => {
  const { data } = await apiClient.get<ChatMessage[]>(`/jobs/${jobId}/chat/history`);
  return data ?? [];
};

import { lazy, Suspense } from 'react';
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Navigate } from 'react-router-dom';
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { ToastProvider } from '@/components/ui/Toast';
import { ProtectedRoute } from '@/components/ProtectedRoute';
import { isLoggedIn } from '@/lib/auth';

const LoginPage = lazy(() => import('@/pages/LoginPage').then((m) => ({ default: m.LoginPage })));
const DashboardPage = lazy(() => import('@/pages/DashboardPage').then((m) => ({ default: m.DashboardPage })));
const UploadPage = lazy(() => import('@/pages/UploadPage').then((m) => ({ default: m.UploadPage })));
const JobDetailPage = lazy(() => import('@/pages/JobDetailPage').then((m) => ({ default: m.JobDetailPage })));

const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  defaultOptions: {
    queries: {
      retry: 1,
      refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
      staleTime: 5_000,
    },
  },
});

export default function App() {
  return (
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <ToastProvider>
        <BrowserRouter>
          <Suspense fallback={<div className="min-h-screen bg-sky-gradient grid place-items-center text-sm text-slate-500">Loading workspace…</div>}>
          <Routes>
            <Route path="/login" element={isLoggedIn() ? <Navigate to="/dashboard" replace /> : <LoginPage />} />
            <Route path="/dashboard" element={<ProtectedRoute><DashboardPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />
            <Route path="/upload"    element={<ProtectedRoute><UploadPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />
            <Route path="/jobs/:jobId" element={<ProtectedRoute><JobDetailPage /></ProtectedRoute>} />
            <Route path="/" element={<Navigate to={isLoggedIn() ? '/dashboard' : '/login'} replace />} />
            <Route path="*" element={<Navigate to="/dashboard" replace />} />
          </Routes>
          </Suspense>
        </BrowserRouter>
      </ToastProvider>
    </QueryClientProvider>
  );
}

END FILE: frontend/src/App.tsx

FILE 57/84
PATH: frontend/src/components/ChatWidget.tsx
